This was with Li Yueru available, so the current frontcourt rotation is weaker than the March baseline.

### China — pre-World Cup preparation

Recorded FIBA preparation results:
- 74-76 Australia
- 82-84 Australia
- 70-67 Nigeria
- 69-65 Nigeria
- 77-88 Nigeria
- 76-88 Italy
- 57-81 Italy
- 71-72 Spain
- plus additional Mali/France preparation entries whose usable final score was not confirmed in the retrieved tracker

Across the eight scored games above:
- **China scored:** 72.0 per game
- **Allowed:** 77.6
- **Combined:** ~149.6
- **Average margin:** about -5.6

Against Australia/Italy/Spain specifically, China lost by:
- 2
- 2
- 12
- 24
- 1

No recorded preparation loss in that high-quality subset reached 30 points.

This is descriptive and preparation-game rotations differ from World Cup rotations, but it is direct evidence against treating +29.5 as automatically unsafe.

---

## 5. Direct USA-China history — downweighted

Official FIBA event history:
- 2022 World Cup Final: USA 83-61 China — margin 22, total 144
- 2022 World Cup Group Phase: USA 77-63 China — margin 14, total 140
- 2018 World Cup: USA 100-88 China — margin 12, total 188
- USA leads the official FIBA H2H **15-0**

The 2022 games both:
- would have cashed **China +29.5**
- would have cashed **Under 161.5**

But current roster continuity is incomplete:
- USA no longer has the same Wilson/Plum-led structure and now has a younger Clark/Bueckers generation;
- China is without Li Yueru and has added Zhang Ziyu.

Therefore old H2H is supporting context, not a governing coefficient.

---

## 6. Possession / efficiency mechanism

No fitted possession model exists, so pace is handled as a qualitative corridor.

### USA offensive process
Primary routes:
- live-ball turnovers -> transition;
- Clark/Gray/Bueckers creation and early offence;
- Stewart/Collier mismatch scoring;
- offensive rebounding from Boston/Reese/Iriafen;
- deep bench maintaining pressure after starter reduction.

USA's key route to **-29.5 + Over** is not simply shooting well. It is:
1. force China turnovers;
2. create transition and paint/free-throw opportunities before China's size is set;
3. maintain offensive quality with the second unit;
4. keep defensive intensity high enough that China's response scoring does not compress the margin.

### China offensive process
Primary routes:
- Han Xu high-post/pop skill and interior finishing;
- Zhang Ziyu rim pressure/size;
- Wang Siyu / Yang Shuyu / Li Yuan ball handling;
- offensive rebounding and half-court post mismatches.

China's main risk is guard pressure:
- if USA disrupts entry passes and creates live-ball turnovers, China's size becomes less valuable because it cannot consistently establish half-court position;
- if China gets clean half-court entries and controls the glass, its scoring floor rises and the 29.5 cushion becomes much stronger.

---

## 7. Team-score budget at 161.5

The active Drive basketball framework requires solving the favourite score needed to cross the total at plausible underdog scores.

| China score | USA needs for **Over 161.5** | Interpretation |
|---:|---:|---|
| **55** | **107** | Requires an extreme USA offensive game |
| **60** | **102** | High USA ceiling needed |
| **65** | **97** | Plausible but above ordinary strong-opponent central scoring |
| **70** | **92** | Very plausible if China sustains offence |
| **75** | **87** | Over becomes easy, but this requires a strong China offensive response |

The total therefore depends heavily on **China's scoring floor**.

A USA blowout is not automatically an Over:
- 96-60 = **156**, USA -29.5 + Under
- 92-61 = **153**, USA -29.5 + Under

And an underdog cover is not automatically an Under:
- 94-70 = **164**, China +29.5 + Over

This prevents spread and total from being treated as independent safety plays.

---

Representative:

- USA **94-66** (160, margin 28)
- USA **95-64** (159, margin 31)

This is the key central family around both supplied thresholds.

Or China shooting/spacing holds:

USA keeps primary creators/defensive pressure active into the second half:
- **100-62**
- **102-64**

Effects:
- USA -29.5 strong
- total can land either side depending on China's floor

### `BK-B4` — favourite slowdown / bench sustain
USA opens a 25–35 point lead, reduces veteran minutes, but bench offence remains competent:
- **96-67**
- **98-65**

Margin compression and total can diverge.

### `BK-B5` — China response
China's size generates offensive rebounds, paint touches and free throws against reduced USA defensive intensity:
- **93-69**

China +29.5 becomes strong; Over becomes live.

### `BK-B6` — China suppression
USA ball pressure disrupts China's guards and entry passes:
- **95-57**
- **99-59**

USA -29.5 + Under 161.5 can win together.

### `BK-B7` — foul / bonus tail
A high foul/free-throw game can push a 156–160 central state through 161.5 without changing the possession thesis much.

### `BK-B8` — overtime
OT is a low-probability tail in a matchup with this winner separation. Operator endpoint is unknown; no settlement assumption is made.

---

## 9. Spread decomposition

### USA -29.5 needs all of:
- opening separation;
- efficient conversion of turnover/rebound advantages;
- China scoring suppression;
- limited second-half garbage-time compression.

The first three are plausible. The fourth is the main uncertainty.

USA's March qualifiers show the ceiling:
- +64 Senegal
- +43 Puerto Rico
- +34 Italy
- +55 New Zealand

But against the best opponent in that field, Spain, USA won only by **14**.

China is FIBA #4 and, despite a poor warm-up record, stayed within 1–2 points of Australia twice and Spain once and within 12 of Italy in one meeting. This materially widens the China-cover branch.

### China +29.5 survives:
- any China upset;
- any USA win by 1–29;
- many ordinary late-margin-compression states.

The primary kill path is a USA defensive avalanche that turns China turnovers into repeated transition points and leaves China's half-court offence below 60.

---

## 10. Ranked forecast

| Rank | Contract | Verdict | Why |
|---:|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Under 161.5 points** | **BEST RELATIVE LEAN** | The total demands either a very high USA score when China is suppressed or a 68–75 point China response. USA's qualifier totals averaged 149.6; China's eight scored warm-ups also averaged about 149.6 combined. Current branches around 92-65, 94-66 and 95-64 all stay Under. Favourite blowout can coexist with Under. |
| **2** | **China +29.5** | **LEAN / close second** | 29.5 is an extreme margin against the world #4 side. China stayed within 24 or less in every scored high-level warm-up retrieved and within 1–2 of Australia/Spain multiple times. Han Xu/Zhang Ziyu provide a frontcourt floor. USA's depth can still break this through turnover/transition pressure. |
| **3** | **USA -29.5** | **LIVE BLOWOUT ALTERNATIVE** | USA's qualifier baseline shows genuine 30–60 point blowout capacity, and China's Li Yueru absence plus backcourt pressure vulnerability create a clear 30+ path. It ranks below China +29.5 because this line needs separation to persist through bench/garbage time against a top-four national team. |
| **4** | **Over 161.5 points** | **LEAST LIKELY, but live** | Over requires roughly USA 97+ if China scores 65, or China 70+ if USA stays near 92. USA's offensive depth can get there, but China's current scoring/guard profile and USA's defensive turnover pressure make the combined upper branch less central. |

### Central qualitative score corridor

Representative central families:
- **USA 92-64** — 156, USA +28 -> China +29.5 / Under
- **USA 94-66** — 160, USA +28 -> China +29.5 / Under
- **USA 96-64** — 160, USA +32 -> USA -29.5 / Under

This is why the Under is ranked above either spread side.

---

## 11. Potential game winner

### **USA — STRONG qualitative winner lean**

Winner confidence is materially stronger than spread confidence.

Reasons:
1. USA is FIBA world #1 and four-time defending World Cup champion.
2. Current 12-player roster retains elite creation, shooting, defence and rebounding even without Wilson/Plum.
3. USA went 5-0 at the March qualifier and has won 30 straight World Cup games entering Berlin.
4. China is in a generational transition and lacks Li Yueru.
5. China's primary roster concern is guard/wing creation, directly vulnerable to USA perimeter pressure.

China's size means the upset is not literally impossible, but it is a tail outcome rather than a central state.

## 2. BK-P1–BK-P4 gate

| Gate | Status |
|---|---|
| `BK-P1` league/clock | PASS — FIBA senior women's rules, four 10-minute quarters |

### Decision-driving availability

**Korea have lost Jisu Park** for the World Cup. This is the most important roster change on the card.

Why it matters:
- 1.98 m center and primary rim/rebounding anchor;
- in Korea's March 77-60 win over Nigeria she had **11 points, 10 rebounds, 4 assists, 4 blocks and +21** in 28 minutes;
- across the March qualifier she averaged **8.4 points, 6.6 rebounds, 3.4 assists and 2.0 blocks**.

Korea's current offence therefore leans more heavily on **Leeseul Kang**, **Jihyun Park**, **Yeeun Heo** and perimeter shot-making, while its interior defence/rebounding becomes materially thinner.

Nigeria's current core remains veteran-heavy:
- **Ezinne Kalu** — lead guard / pressure / creation
- **Victoria Macaulay** — interior scoring/size
- **Murjanatu Musa** — rebounding/interior activity
- **Amy Okonkwo** — scoring forward
- support from Promise Amukamara, Elizabeth Balogun, Pallas Kunaiyi-Akpanah and other established internationals

FIBA's current team profile specifically flags Macaulay and Musa as a potent paint duo and Kalu as the heartbeat of the team.

---

- Nigeria: **FIBA world #8**
- Korea: **FIBA world #15**
- FIBA's final pre-tournament Smart Power Rankings placed Nigeria **#10** and Korea **#13**.
- Nigeria's preparation form was poor enough for FIBA to describe the team as unpredictable.
- Korea's major downgrade is the loss of Jisu Park, described by FIBA as a "hammer-blow."

This is not used as a numerical coefficient; it frames the current roster/quality regime.

## 5. Direct March rematch — highly relevant but roster-adjusted

**Korea 77-60 Nigeria**, 12 March 2026.

Quarter scores:
- Q1: 20-16 Korea
- Q2: 16-16
- Q3: 22-19 Korea
- Q4: 19-9 Korea

Game-level process:
- Korea shot **40% FG** vs Nigeria **36%**
- Korea shot **57.7% on 2PT** vs Nigeria **40%**
- Korea's 3PT rate was not exceptional: **28.2%** vs Nigeria **23.5%**
- Korea's biggest lead reached 19
- Jihyun Park was the principal scorer
- Jisu Park's interior/rebounding/defensive impact was material

### How to use it now

The 17-point Korea win is **not reusable as a straight H2H coefficient** because Jisu Park is absent.

But it still matters:
- Korea already showed it can handle Nigeria's pressure and physicality;
- Nigeria's offence was held to 60;
- Nigeria now gains a major interior matchup improvement without Jisu.

This creates a much closer present-day spread tree than "Nigeria #8 vs Korea #15" alone suggests.

## 7. Possession / shot-profile mechanism

### Nigeria
Likely strengths:
- physical ball pressure
- live-ball turnovers into transition
- offensive rebounding
- Kalu point-of-attack pressure and creation
- Macaulay/Musa/Okonkwo paint and second-chance scoring

Main upside mechanism:
`Korea turnover -> Nigeria transition -> offensive glass / paint foul pressure -> repeat high-value possessions`

### Korea
Likely strengths:
- Leeseul Kang high-volume three-point shooting
- Jihyun Park all-around creation/scoring
- Yeeun Heo playmaking
- ability to stretch a defence if given clean catch-and-shoot windows

Main resistance mechanism:
`secure ball -> avoid live-ball turnovers -> generate threes -> force Nigeria into half-court offence`

### Jisu Park absence
This shifts:
- defensive rebounding toward Nigeria
- paint deterrence toward Nigeria
- foul-trouble resilience toward Nigeria
- Korea's half-court facilitation slightly downward

But it can also push Korea toward a more perimeter-heavy/high-variance attack, widening both its shooting-upside and shooting-collapse branches.

## 10. Spread decomposition

### Nigeria -12.5 requires
1. Nigeria to win the turnover/transition battle;
2. Nigeria to exploit Korea's weakened interior/rebounding;
3. Korea's threes not to run hot;
4. separation to survive the fourth quarter.

### Korea +12.5 survives
- any Korea win;
- any Nigeria win by 1-12;
- a meaningful portion of the central "Nigeria better, Korea still shoots enough" tree.

The strongest argument for the cushion is that:
- Korea beat Nigeria by 17 in March;
- Nigeria's August preparation was poor;
- FIBA itself moved Nigeria down to #10 in its current power ranking.

The strongest argument against it is Jisu Park's absence, which removes the exact player who was +21 with a 10-rebound/4-block performance in the March matchup.

## 12. Potential winner

### **Nigeria — moderate qualitative lean**

Reasons:
1. Nigeria is FIBA world #8 vs Korea #15.
2. Korea has lost its most important interior defender/rebounder, Jisu Park.

### Endpoint clarification

The user market calls the six-over checkpoint the "powerplay". In a rain-reduced T20 the official fielding-restriction powerplay can be recalculated. This card therefore models the exact **six-over cumulative-run endpoint** without falsely asserting that all six overs remain the regulatory powerplay.

## 4. Rain-reduced scoring geometry

### 12-over line: 120.5
To score 121:
- **121 / 12 = 10.08 runs per over**

### 6-over line: 57.5
To score 58:
- **58 / 6 = 9.67 runs per over**

The six-over checkpoint consumes **half of the shortened innings**, so Belfast can attack earlier than in a normal T20, but a wicket cluster leaves almost no rebuilding time.

## 8. Phase-to-innings transition at the exact lines

If Belfast is **52/2 after six**, it needs **69 from the last six** to reach 121:
- **11.50 rpo**

If Belfast is **58/1 after six**, it needs **63 from the last six**:
- **10.50 rpo**

If Belfast is **62/1 after six**, it needs **59 from the last six**:
- **9.83 rpo**

Therefore **Over 57.5 at six does not imply Over 120.5 at 12**. A 60/1 start can still finish 116–119 if Glasgow controls overs 7–9.

# P-287 — Alexander Bublik vs Tommy Paul — 2026 US Open

## Frozen state
- Event: US Open men's singles, Round 3
- Court: Louis Armstrong Stadium, outdoor hard
- Format: best-of-five
- Cutoff: **2026-09-05 02:06:59 AEST / 2026-09-04 12:06:59 EDT**
- Official US Open SlamTracker state at cutoff: **UPCOMING**
- Method: `MDS-2026.09.04-v3.4 + GFA-2 + SFA-TENNIS`
- Retirement/walkover terms: `UNKNOWN_DEFINITION`

## Exact supplied slate
1. Bublik +2.5 games
2. Paul -2.5 games
3. Over 40.5 total games
4. Under 40.5 total games

## Current tournament / workload
**Bublik**
- R1: beat J.J. Wolf 6-4, 6-2, 3-6, 6-1 — 34 games
- R2: beat Adrian Mannarino 6-3, 6-1, 6-1 — 23 games
- R2 specialist stats: 16 aces, 3 DFs, 73% first serves in, 91% first-serve points won, 53% second-serve points won
- Tournament exposure: **57 games**

**Paul**
- R1: beat Coleman Wong 6-7, 6-1, 6-3, 6-3 — 38 games
- R2: beat Dino Prizmic 6-2, 6-3, 5-7, 6-4 — 39 games, about 3h04
- R2 specialist stats: 9 aces, 4 DFs, 54% first serves in, 83% first-serve points won, 48% second-serve points won
- Tournament exposure: **77 games**

Bublik is substantially fresher through two rounds; Paul has the stronger North American hard-court lead-in.

## Current hard-court regime
Paul reached the Cincinnati quarterfinal and beat Alexander Zverev 4-6, 7-6(6), 6-4, showing strong late return pressure and second-serve attack. Bublik did not play Montreal/Cincinnati; the US Open was his first North American summer hard-court event, but he has served exceptionally well through two rounds.

## H2H continuity
Official US Open SlamTracker lists Paul leading the career H2H **3-1**.

Highest-continuity meeting:
- **2025 US Open R3:** Bublik d. Paul 7-6, 6-7, 6-4, 6-7, 6-1
- 55 total games, three tiebreaks
- Paul's 2025 medical timeout means the fifth-set 6-1 margin is not reused as a clean coefficient.

Other useful meetings:
- 2025 Miami hard: Paul won 5-7, 7-5, 6-4; Bublik lost by only two games
- 2024 Wimbledon: Paul won 6-3, 6-4, 6-2; lower surface continuity
- 2021 Rotterdam: Paul won in three; old indoor-hard meeting, heavily downweighted

## Serve/return matchup
- Bublik owns the bigger first-strike serve/ace ceiling and current R2 serving form.
- Paul owns the more stable return/second-serve pressure and has stronger recent high-level North American hard evidence.
- Bublik's second serve/double-fault volatility is Paul's main separation route.
- Paul's lower first-serve percentage in R2 creates a Bublik set-winning route if Bublik keeps return games short and protects his own serve.

## Environment
NWS around noon in Flushing: about 27°C/81°F, light NW wind around 6 mph, low near-term rain risk. Louis Armstrong has a retractable roof; exact roof state was not confirmed. No strong environment sign is applied.

## Best-of-five set-count mixture
Qualitative pre-total mixture:
- 3-set branch: meaningful but subordinate
- 4-set branch: largest branch
- 5-set branch: material
The combination of Bublik serve resistance, Paul's return quality, both players already dropping sets, and the 2025 same-site five-set meeting increases 4/5-set mass.

## Representative score tree
- Bublik 3-0: 7-6, 6-4, 7-6 = 33 → Bublik +2.5 / Under
- Bublik 3-1: 7-6, 4-6, 7-6, 6-4 = 43 → Bublik +2.5 / Over
- Bublik 3-2: 7-6, 4-6, 7-6, 4-6, 6-4 = 50 → Bublik +2.5 / Over
- Paul 3-0: 6-4, 7-6, 6-4 = 33 → Paul -2.5 / Under
- Paul 3-1: 7-6, 6-4, 4-6, 6-4 = 43 → Over; game handicap near boundary
- Paul 3-2: 6-7, 7-6, 6-4, 4-6, 6-4 = 49 → Over; Bublik +2.5 remains live depending cumulative margin

| Rank | Contract | Verdict | Reason |
|---:|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Over 40.5 games** | **BEST RELATIVE LEAN** | Large four-set branch plus material five-set branch. Bublik's serve/tiebreak ceiling and Paul's return resilience make set trading plausible. Same-site 2025 meeting reached 55 games. Kill path: straight sets or compact 3-1 at 38-40 games. |
| **2** | **Bublik +2.5 games** | **LEAN** | Covers all Bublik wins plus some narrow Paul wins. Bublik is fresher by 20 tournament games, in elite current serving form, and this handicap would have survived both relevant 2025 outdoor-hard meetings. |

### Kade Morris
Current MLB: 0-1, 9.82 ERA, 14.2 IP, 10 K, 2.11 WHIP in only five appearances and one prior MLB start. The Bulldogs remain competitive around the ball, but Blackburn's absence removes an experienced midfield/leadership piece and De Berry's absence weakens structural depth. Gavalas' return helps, but does not fully replace both losses.

Sydney projects to generate the larger inside-50/scoring-shot budget. The question for -10.5 is whether that territory edge converts into 11+ points rather than a close low-scoring win.

## 7. Corner process

SFA-SOCCER requires corners to be treated separately from goals.

Available current evidence:
- Juárez roughly **7.7 shots per game**
- Pachuca roughly **14.5 shots per game**
- Juárez dangerous attacks around **37.7/game**
- Pachuca around **42.5/game**
- current corner projection source expects approximately **4 Juárez corners to 6 Pachuca**
- combined corner environment around **9.6**

Recent Juárez corner results include:
- vs Puebla: **5-7**
- at Chivas: **2-19**
- at Monterrey: **0-10**
- vs América: **5-7**
- at Toluca: **3-5**

This strongly supports a **Pachuca corner-share advantage** more than a precise total-corners line.

Why not promote a total-corners Over?
- current 9.5 projection is almost 50/50
- no exact operator line was supplied
- score-state can suppress Pachuca corners if they lead early
- Juárez trailing may inflate their late corner share

Therefore the best derivative is:
### **Pachuca most corners / corner race**

Evidence cap: `FORCED RANK / MEDIUM-LOW` because the exact provider/settlement line was not supplied.

## 2. Governing tennis controls applied

The Drive's active tennis framework requires:
- best-of-five to remain a separate population from best-of-three;
- current surface and tournament regime to outrank generic career reputation;
- serve, return, set-count and cumulative-game-margin processes to remain linked;
- winner and game handicap to be treated as different contracts;
- retirement/walkover rules to be frozen when supplied; here they were not;
- H2H to be weighted by format, surface, recency and participant continuity rather than copied mechanically.

The current Drive authority remains `PREDICTION_LOG_COMBINED_2.md`; the local mirror continues sequentially because Drive is read-only.

---

## 3. Current ranking and broad current regime

Current ranking sources around the event:
- **Ben Shelton: ATP #9**
- **Denis Shapovalov: ATP #48**

Shelton is seeded **No. 8** at this US Open.

The ranking gap favours Shelton as winner, but the current H2H and serve profiles make a 6+ cumulative-game win materially more demanding than the moneyline.

---

## 4. Current US Open path and workload

### Ben Shelton

Round 1:
- beat Tallon Griekspoor **1-6, 6-1, 7-6(3), 6-2**
- total games: **35**

Round 2:
- beat Hubert Hurkacz **6-3, 5-7, 7-6(3), 7-5**
- total games: **46**

Tournament exposure through two rounds:
- **81 games**
- two four-set matches
- one tiebreak in each match

Official US Open reporting notes Shelton hit **148 mph** in Round 2, the fastest recorded serve of the tournament through two rounds.

Against Hurkacz:
- Shelton had to solve another elite-serving opponent;
- the match remained close deep into the fourth set;
- Shelton saved break pressure and created the decisive late break.

This is strong evidence for Shelton's current ability to navigate close service-dominated sets, but it is not evidence that every set will separate by multiple games.

### Denis Shapovalov

Round 1:
- beat Miomir Kecmanovic **6-4, 4-6, 6-4, 6-1**
- total games: **37**

Round 2:
- beat Luca Van Assche **6-0, 6-4, 7-6(7)**
- total games: **29**

Tournament exposure:
- **66 games**

Shapovalov therefore enters with roughly **15 fewer games of match exposure** than Shelton through two rounds.

Current physical caveat:
- Shapovalov retired from Montreal on 4 August with an ankle injury suffered around the Los Cabos final;
- he subsequently completed a close three-set Cincinnati match and has completed two US Open wins;
- no current match-day medical limitation was found.
This is functional evidence of recovery, not a formal medical clearance.

---

## 5. North American hard-court form

### Shelton

Shelton's strongest current external evidence:
- defended the Montreal Masters 1000 title;
- beat Brandon Nakashima **6-3, 7-6(4)** in the final;
- won the entire Montreal event **without dropping a set across six matches**;
- rose to World No. 6 after that title before later ranking movement.

He then lost his Cincinnati opener to Jaime Faria **6-4, 6-4**, a reminder that his high ceiling still has lower-level volatility.

### Shapovalov

Shapovalov:
- reached the Los Cabos final;
- beat Cameron Norrie **6-3, 5-7, 6-4** in the semifinal;
- then carried an ankle injury into Montreal and retired;
- returned in Cincinnati and lost to Rafael Jodar **7-5, 4-6, 7-5** after leading 5-1 in the third.

This is a mixed current regime:
- high first-strike ceiling;
- enough physical/competitive evidence to extend strong opponents;
- still meaningful volatility and game-management risk.

---

## 6. Official H2H continuity

Official US Open sources list:
- **Shelton leads 4-0**

Important detail:
- **three of the four meetings went to a deciding set**
- **all but one included a tiebreak**

### 2026 Dallas — indoor hard
Shelton won:
- **4-6, 6-4, 7-6(4)**
- cumulative game margin: **Shelton +1**
- total: **33 games** in best-of-three

Shapovalov created 11 break points; Shelton saved 10 of them. This was a highly competitive match.

### 2024 Shanghai — outdoor hard
Shelton won:
- **6-3, 7-5**
- cumulative game margin: **Shelton +5**

This is highly relevant to today's **+5.5** line:
Shapovalov would have covered +5.5 despite losing in straight sets.

### 2024 Washington — outdoor hard
Shelton was leading:
- **7-6(5), 6-6**, 6-3 in the second-set tiebreak
when Shapovalov was defaulted after an exchange with a spectator.

The official H2H counts this as a Shelton win, but it is **not a completed performance result** and must not be used as a clean margin/total observation.

### 2024 Wimbledon — grass, best-of-five
Shelton won:
- **6-7(4), 6-2, 6-4, 4-6, 6-2**
- total: **49 games**
- cumulative game margin: **Shelton +7**

This is the highest **format continuity** meeting but lower **surface continuity** than today's hard court.

### H2H conclusion

The four-match record strongly supports Shelton as the winner lean.

But the shape of the H2H supports Shapovalov's game cushion:
- Dallas: +1 Shelton
- Shanghai: +5 Shelton
- Washington: incomplete, very close through nearly two sets
- Wimbledon: +7 Shelton in five

A 5.5-game line sits directly inside the historical separation corridor rather than outside it.

---

## 7. Serve / return interaction

Both are left-handed first-strike players.

### Shelton
Strengths:
- elite serve speed and free-point creation;
- current US Open serve confidence;
- superior current ranking and recent hard-court results;
- improving ability to return big servers after Griekspoor/Hurkacz.

Primary separation mechanism:
`big first serve -> short return games -> Shapovalov second-serve pressure/errors -> one break per set -> cumulative margin`

### Shapovalov
Strengths:
- explosive lefty serve;
- one-handed backhand capable of early first-strike aggression;
- enough return aggression to create break chances against Shelton;
- current freshness advantage.

Primary cushion/extension mechanism:
`hold-heavy set -> tiebreak or 7-5 set -> steal one set -> cumulative game margin remains compressed`

Primary risk:
- double-fault / aggressive-error clusters;
- scoreboard volatility after losing a close set;
- Shelton's superior recent consistency can turn one bad service stretch into a 6-2 or 6-3 set.

## 9. Best-of-five set-count / total-games structure

The supplied total is **38.5**.

This line does not require five sets, but it does require the four-set branch to avoid being too compact.

### Over examples
- Shelton 7-6, 6-4, 4-6, 7-6 = **46**
- Shelton 6-4, 7-6, 4-6, 6-4 = **42**
- Shelton 7-5, 4-6, 7-6, 6-3 = **44**

### Under examples
- Shelton 6-3, 6-4, 6-4 = **29**
- Shelton 7-6, 6-4, 6-3 = **32**
- Shelton 6-3, 6-4, 4-6, 6-3 = **38**

The current central tree is:
- Shelton 3-0: meaningful
- Shelton 3-1: largest single branch
- Shelton 3-2: material but smaller
- Shapovalov win: live but subordinate

The 38.5 line therefore sits near the compact-four-set / ordinary-four-set boundary.

---

## 10. Cross-market score tree

### `TE-B1` Shelton close straight sets
Example:
- **7-6, 6-4, 6-4**
- total 33
- Shelton game margin +5

Results:
- **Shapovalov +5.5**
- Under 38.5
- Shelton winner

This is important: Shapovalov +5.5 can survive even a Shelton 3-0.

### `TE-B2` Shelton separated straight sets
Example:
- **6-3, 6-3, 6-4**
- total 28
- Shelton +8

Results:
- Shelton -5.5
- Under 38.5

### `TE-B3` central competitive four-set Shelton win
Example:
- **7-6, 6-4, 4-6, 7-6**
- total 46
- Shelton +2

Results:
- **Shapovalov +5.5**
- **Over 38.5**
- Shelton winner

This is the main central branch.

### `TE-B4` separated four-set Shelton win
Example:
- **6-3, 6-4, 4-6, 6-3**
- total 38
- Shelton +6

Results:
- Shelton -5.5
- Under 38.5

This is the principal alternative branch.

### `TE-B5` five-set extension
Most ordinary five-set score shapes are well above 38.5 and usually keep the game margin compressed unless one set is a blowout.

### `TE-B6` Shapovalov upset
Any Shapovalov win automatically cashes +5.5; most four/five-set upset branches also support Over.

### `TE-B8` retirement
Unknown operator rules. Current pregame medical evidence does not justify assuming a retirement.

## 12. Potential match winner

### **Ben Shelton — moderate-to-strong qualitative lean**

Why:

## 6. Current personnel mechanisms

### North midfield / territory
Key chain:
`Rennie / ruck contest -> Garner + Riddell + Sheerin -> front-half lock -> King / Shierlaw / Randall / Bogue / O'Loughlin`

Current evidence:
- Ash Riddell had 42 disposals against Brisbane.
- Jasmine Garner had 31 in that game.
- North repeatedly won field position through contest and clearance pressure.
- Sheerin's return adds another experienced contest/transition piece.

Mia King's season-ending absence reduces midfield rotation depth, but it has not materially changed North's ability to control territory through three rounds.

### North forward line
Recent contributions are distributed:
- Bogue, O'Loughlin and Shierlaw kicked two each against the Bulldogs.
- Emma King remains a difficult aerial matchup and plays her 100th AFLW game.
- Tahlia Randall remains a strong forward/ruck scoring threat.

This lowers dependence on one single goal scorer.

### St Kilda
Key resistance chain:
`Tyanna Smith / Georgia Patrikios / Serene Watson -> stoppage parity -> Tawhiao-Wardlaw contested marking -> scoring conversion`

St Kilda actually generated periods of dominance against Sydney, especially early and late, but finished **1.9** and failed to convert.

Tawhiao-Wardlaw remains a legitimate high-end marking forward, but the current season conversion/output has been poor.

### AFL weather treatment

Wind/showers are **bidirectional**:
- can reduce set-shot accuracy and long-kick efficiency;
- can reduce clean marks / forward connection;
- can create repeat territory, turnovers and short-field scoring.

For this matchup, the more likely directional effect is:
- **lower conversion efficiency**
- especially harmful to the weaker St Kilda attack

That modestly supports **Under 89.5**, but weather is not used as a stand-alone Under trigger.

St Kilda's 2026 loss margins:

Only **1 of 3** has exceeded 51.5.

These are tiny descriptive samples, not cover probabilities, but they demonstrate the line sits in the upper tail rather than the ordinary centre.

The official AFLW Round 4 preview tipped North by **53**, almost exactly on the market threshold. That reinforces the conclusion that the spread itself is finely balanced, not a strong favourite handicap.

| **2** | **St Kilda +51.5** | **LEAN / extreme-cushion value** | North are vastly more likely to win, but 51.5 is a huge margin. | **3** | **North Melbourne -51.5** | **LIVE BLOWOUT ALTERNATIVE** | North's scoring-shot edge is enormous, St Kilda are 0-3 with a 34.3 percentage, and Geelong already beat them by 85. | **4** | **Over 89.5 points** | **LEAST LIKELY, but live through North ceiling** | Over mainly needs North itself to score 70–80+ or St Kilda to contribute 25–30. North's 100-point R1 shows the ceiling, and St Kilda's first two games both had totals above 100. It ranks fourth because St Kilda's own scoring floor is extremely low, North's defence is stronger this week, and conditions are not ideal for clean conversion. |

Reasons:

Why winner confidence is much stronger than -51.5 confidence:

- St Kilda showed against Sydney that it can create enough territory to slow a game despite poor finishing;
- extreme margins are more conversion-sensitive than winner markets.

- **North 60–68**

## Detailed retrospective — all ten newly settled events

The grades distinguish a verified process defect from an unidentifiable probability error. `COMPLIANT` below is scoped to the named mechanism, not a certification that every framework field was present. `INCONCLUSIVE` means the frozen record cannot establish the proposed causal/weighting claim. No result is labelled a calibration error from one qualitative observation.

### LOCAL-GEELONG-20260904 — #1 right, #2 wrong

| Question | Finding |
|---|---|
| What went right? | Geelong +11.5 and the Geelong winner annotation were supported by the forward-half/territory mechanism and rest advantage already identified before issue. Geelong won by 33. The final scoring ledger contains 37 Geelong scoring shots versus Carlton’s 19, consistent with sustained opportunity advantage. These are goals plus behinds, not a claim of 37 independently tracked shot attempts. |
| What went wrong? | Under 165.5 lost at 181. The card’s central total corridor was already 158–174, crossing the line, and its high branch included 176–190+. The analysis acknowledged uncertainty, so this is not an undisclosed extreme. It nevertheless used finals/defensive-control language to order Under above Over without a quantified, supported reason that the shot-volume branch should remain subordinate. |
| Actual mechanism | Carlton led 27–11 at quarter time and 37–34 at half-time. Geelong outscored Carlton 39–13 in the third quarter, then 34–24 in the fourth. The second half produced 110 points despite reported wet conditions. Geelong converted only 14 of its 37 scoring shots into goals: the Over did not require Geelong to kick unusually accurately. |
| Improvement | Preserve the successful territory/rest read, but translate it into each team’s shot-volume and conversion budget. Include **high volume with mediocre conversion**, not only “high scoring if conversion runs hot.” At 37 scoring shots, Geelong can score 107 with only 14 goals; a weather-related conversion reduction need not overcome a large opportunity increase. Use L-037/L-047/L-048 and AFL’s existing shot-chain control. |
| Grade | Side/winner mechanism: COMPLIANT. Total direction: INCONCLUSIVE weighting; the line-crossing corridor needs the existing LOW/forced-order disclosure. No numerical downweight of finals or wet weather is estimated here. |

The correct lower-ranked Over is evidence that a plausible failure route was present, not that the card successfully selected the total direction. Carlton -11.5 was the wrong separation direction because Carlton’s early advantage did not survive Geelong’s second-half opportunity advantage. It is reasonable to retain the distinction between a viable Carlton win and the additional 12-point requirement; the 33-point Geelong margin does not establish that future cushions are inherently safe. [Official AFL report](https://www.afl.com.au/news/1601410/geelong-cats-v-carlton-blues-match-report).

### P-280 — both top picks lost; decisive pregame information was missed

| Question | Finding |
|---|---|
| What went right? | Souths were correctly named the likely winner. The card recognised a depleted Roosters side, a possession-collapse branch and the possibility that Souths could drive the total almost alone. Rabbitohs -22.5 and Over 54.5 therefore had an identified path. |
| What went wrong? | Rank #1 Roosters +22.5 rested partly on Collins/Leniu/Watson providing resistance and on Souths starting with an unconventional halves arrangement. The official preview, updated at 18:39 AEST, said Collins withdrew 90 minutes before kickoff and Walker would enter after Arrow’s ceremonial kickoff appearance. Both were available before the 19:51:18 cutoff. An “incomplete handshake” caveat did not repair two decision-driving premises. |
| Actual mechanism | NRL’s final is **50–20**, total 70, not the ABC headline’s 50–22. Souths had 60% possession, completed 40/47 sets against 20/32, and led run metres 1,890–1,081 and line breaks 12–4. Roosters missed 53 tackles to Souths’ 23. These observations support a sustained field-position/defensive-workload failure. They do not isolate Collins’ individual causal contribution. |
| Improvement | At the final-team gate, reconcile every named decision-driving player and their actual expected entry/minutes against the same-day update. A ceremonial lineup label cannot supply a full opening-phase playmaking deficit. Remove unavailable Collins exposure and rebuild Roosters’ middle/bench resistance; then recompute both total and margin from the same two-team budgets. Do not simply flip the prediction after seeing the result. |
| Grade | PROCESS_DEFECT — STATE_FRESHNESS / AVAILABILITY_EXPOSURE on the side and shared score tree. Exact 30-point defeat was unknowable; the stale personnel and role inputs were knowable. L-002/L-038/L-042/L-065 and G31 already required the correction. |

The Under also failed because weakening one attack did not cap the opponent’s scoring. Souths’ 50 alone left only four Roosters points of Under budget. Roosters scored 20, so this was not a need for two exceptional attacks. The official scoring sequence shows the total crossed 54.5 with the 71st-minute Souths try, before the paired 72nd-minute sin bins. Blaming those bins for the original crossing would be a chronology error. Their effects on the final score are separate.

The top two were correlated through an exaggerated resistance/organisation story: neither a large cushion nor a lower Roosters scoring expectation supplied an independent defence against Souths’ repeated opportunities. Retain the winner and blowout-path recognition, while treating the final refresh failure as an actionable defect. [NRL final and statistics](https://www.nrl.com/draw/nrl-premiership/2026/round-27/rabbitohs-v-roosters/), [timestamped NRL team news](https://www.nrl.com/news/2026/09/01/rabbitohs-v-roosters-trell-set-to-start-robbo-rests-stars/).

### P-281 — #1 Under failed before the bullpen; #2 won through a different mechanism

| Question | Finding |
|---|---|
| What went right? | Fubon +1.5 won. The card retained Fubon’s home advantage and an upset route, and correctly noted that the home ninth may be unplayed. The final had only eight Fubon batting innings. A low total can support a cushion, but that was not the mechanism of this 13–0 result. |
| What went wrong? | Rank #1 Under 6.5 and the Rakuten winner rested heavily on Fernández/威能帝’s suppression profile. The named principal kill paths concentrated on Fubon starter 江國豪’s baserunners/early hook, clusters and extras. The actual starter asymmetry ran in the opposite direction. The alternative of Fubon damaging the favoured ace needed its own explicit runs/hook/defence budget. |
| Actual mechanism | Fubon scored 2, 3 and 2 in innings two through four: the total had crossed by the fourth. Fernández allowed 9 hits and 7 runs, 5 earned, in 4 innings despite no walks. Fubon finished with 19 hits, no home runs; Rakuten committed two errors. 江國豪 supplied 7 scoreless innings, with 6 hits and one walk allowed. |
| Improvement | Test each starter’s ordinary adverse contact/defence branch symmetrically. Separate run quality from length and from relief availability. A good K/BB baseline does not eliminate hits-in-sequence or unearned runs; no home-run requirement should be attached to a seven-run threshold. Identify lineup, defence and catcher uncertainty instead of letting a probable-starter comparison carry the entire ranking. |
| Grade | PROCESS_DEFECT in the asymmetric adverse stress budget; AVAILABILITY_EXPOSURE limitations were disclosed and caps retained. Exact bad-start weighting is INCONCLUSIVE because no frozen probabilities exist. Do not claim a pitcher’s single poor outing disproves his prior skill. |

The 13–0 endpoint supports neither the pregame narrow Rakuten-win centre nor the close low-total explanation for Fubon +1.5. It supports the contract while overturning its principal rationale: **result right, mechanism different**. Rakuten -1.5 needed both Rakuten run production and Fubon suppression; both failed. Over 6.5 won through early accumulation against the preferred starter, not extras or a weather stoppage. The later bullpen damage widened an already lost Under.

Previous lessons already contain the necessary pieces: L-023/L-033 for material branches, L-037 for separate team budgets, L-047 for line robustness, and baseball controls 14/19 on defensive and cluster risk. The new step is a symmetric starter-by-starter worked audit, not an automatic CPBL Over bias after two blowouts. The native-language report gives useful sequencing and historical context; its account of Fernández’s prior success is not a reason the reversal was “due.” [Official CPBL box](https://stats.cpbl.com.tw/schedule/2026-A-307), [Liberty Times reporting](https://sports.ltn.com.tw/news/breakingnews/5563625).

### P-282 — #1 Under failed through two separate scoring clusters

| Question | Finding |
|---|---|
| What went right? | Uni +1.5 won, and the Dykxhoorn suppression mechanism was real: 7 scoreless innings, 3 hits and one walk allowed. The pregame warning that a low total can coexist with two-run separation was logically correct, although it had been illustrated mainly with TSG ahead. |
| What went wrong? | Under 6.5 and the TSG winner were built around a low-run contest between two good starters. Uni scored four in the first. The Under survived until a six-run seventh, which exposed the missing linkage between a tiring starter, inherited runners, replacement quality and a same-inning hit cluster. |
| Actual mechanism | Uni’s scoring innings were 4 in the first, 1 in the fifth, 6 in the seventh. Stout worked 6⅔ innings, allowing 9 hits, 3 walks and 8 runs, 7 earned. His immediate reliever recorded no outs, allowing 3 hits and 3 runs. Uni finished with 14 hits; TSG had four. The native report identifies early error/home-run damage and a seventh-inning chain of hits. |
| Improvement | Model an **adverse long start**, not just an early hook. Pass base occupancy and outs into the relief state; charged runs and pitcher-on-mound scoring are different fields. Stress the favourite’s contact/walk/defence branch as fully as the opponent’s. Two strong starter season summaries cannot by themselves protect a low 6.5 full-game threshold. |
| Grade | PROCESS_DEFECT in completeness of the joint starter-to-relief stress budget; exact probability error remains INCONCLUSIVE. Declared incomplete final orders were honest; their absence cannot be converted into confirmed lineup evidence. L-006/L-039/L-058 and BB controls 15/19 apply. |

This is a different failure from P-281. There, seven runs were reached within four innings against the preferred starter. Here, a five-run game became an eleven-run game in the seventh. It would be inaccurate to group both simply as “bullpen collapses,” or to explain this one as rain causing a short Stout outing. No retrieved field established an in-game rain interruption as the cause, and 6⅔ innings is not an early exit.

Uni’s low opponent score was useful to the winning cushion, but **0–11 is a separation state, not a close-game state**. The runner-up pick winning is not validation of the TSG winner call. Keep the successful Dykxhoorn-quality read and improve the two-sided game tree and transition accounting. [Official CPBL box](https://stats.cpbl.com.tw/schedule/2026-A-308), [Videoland report](https://news.videoland.com.tw/article/8ebd4097-f67e-448d-b187-bfcd98f10534.html).

### P-283 — successful toss refresh; the full-innings Under did not survive retained wickets

| Question | Finding |
|---|---|
| What went right? | The final refresh correctly removed the chase-censoring branch when South Africa were confirmed batting first and moved first-six Over 53.5 to #1. South Africa reached 70/0 after six, and the South Africa winner was correct by 11 runs. The exact early/full target distinction was preserved. |
| What went wrong? | #2 Under 183.5 lost at 228/4. It still relied on a five-match first-innings venue mean of 168.6 after the target exposure changed. The refreshed central range 174–188 already straddled the threshold and the supported retained-wicket branch crossed it. Naming that branch was useful; no evidence established a robust Under preference. |
| Actual mechanism | South Africa were 70/0 after six, 127/0 after ten and 187/2 after fifteen. The last five added 41. The 183.5 line was beaten before the death overs ended, rather than requiring an exceptional last-over rescue. Pretorius and Esterhuizen opened with 156; they scored 101 and 88 respectively. |
| Improvement | At each candidate six-over state, calculate the remaining Under allowance and propagate batter identity, wickets, balls and opposing bowler allocation. From actual 70/0 the maximum Under-compatible remainder was 113 from 84 legal balls, about 8.07 per over. That is a retrospective diagnostic, not a permissible pregame live input. Pregame states such as 60/0 need the same calculation before ranking. |
| Grade | Toss-refresh and #1 mechanism: COMPLIANT. Full-innings ordering: INCONCLUSIVE probability/weighting, with an existing LOW evidence and conditional-budget requirement. The original pre-toss #1 is superseded and must not be counted as another failure. |

The important lesson is not that an early Over forces an innings Over. It is that the same phase-end state must feed both. Here the opening partnership remained intact far beyond the powerplay, and scoring accelerated in the middle before moderating late. A “death-overs explosion” explanation would miss where the boundary was crossed. Namibia’s 217/3 also shows that an SA batting total and an SA winner are separate targets; this was a narrow defence of a large score, not a dominant low-chase bowling result. Retain the completed pitch-search ladder and the toss correction, improve L-039/L-050/L-057 phase conditioning, and keep the five-match venue average as sparse context. [ICC final/report](https://www.icc-cricket.com/news/proteas-edge-namibia-to-secure-final-clash-against-zimbabwe), [six-over and over-by-over fields](https://sports.ndtv.com/cricket/nam-sa-over-comparison/nam-vs-sa-match-6-windhoek-nasa09042026273845).

### P-284 — #1 Under was right; a weak opponent can also lose a large cushion

| Question | Finding |
|---|---|
| What went right? | Under 161.5 won at 155, and USA were correctly named the winner. The card explicitly represented USA -29.5 plus Under states; it did not mechanically equate a blowout with an Over. That distinction was useful. |
| What went wrong? | China +29.5 lost by 3.5 points relative to the threshold. Rank #2 relied partly on China’s ranking, competitive warm-ups, size and late compression. USA’s depth and China suppression persisted instead. The frozen “principal kill path” mentioned China below 60, but China scored 61 and still failed to cover: a rounded verbal boundary was too restrictive. |
| Actual mechanism | Quarter scoring was USA 27/22/21/24, China 14/15/13/19. The lead grew from 20 at half-time to 28 after three and 33 at full-time; the last quarter extended it by five. China shot about 32% overall against USA’s 45%. The box supports shooting suppression, not a proved low-possession game. |
| Improvement | Express the spread boundary algebraically. At USA 94, China need at least 65 for +29.5; Under 161.5 allows China at most 67. Both can win in 65–67, but 61 produces Under plus USA cover. Map bench offence and opponent response separately; do not assume reduced starter exposure produces margin compression. |
| Grade | Total mechanism: COMPLIANT. Spread weighting: INCONCLUSIVE; supported adverse branch occurred. Exact boundary statement needs a SOURCE_TRANSFORMATION/arithmetic repair. L-037/L-043/L-048 and G20.1 apply. |

USA -29.5 won because separation persisted through all four quarters; Over lost because China’s contribution was too small. The result reinforces the pregame decision to place total above either spread direction, while exposing why the runner-up was not nearly as robust as the phrase “extreme margin against world #4” suggested. A warm-up result is not automatically comparable in opponent, role/minutes, stakes or substitution pattern. No claim is made that China’s ranking or recent preparation should now receive a fitted penalty. [Official FIBA score and statistics](https://www.fiba.basketball/en/events/fiba-womens-basketball-world-cup-2026/games/128134-USA-CHN).

### P-285 — deep #1 failure; the underdog’s offence was not allowed to win the modelled game

| Question | Finding |
|---|---|
| What went right? | Korea +12.5 won, and the card recognised perimeter scoring plus Nigeria’s poor preparation as reasons to retain the cushion. The Korea scoring response was directionally right. The problem was its scale and its failure to reach the winner/separation tree. |
| What went wrong? | Under 143.5 lost at 180 and the Nigeria winner was wrong. Every numerical central, shooting, response and favourite branch printed in the frozen tree still had Nigeria winning. Even the Korea hot-shooting example was Nigeria 76–70. An outright Korea win was covered in the contract definition but not given a representative two-sided score budget. This repeats the structural omission noted at P-270. |
| Actual mechanism | Korea led 48–45 at half-time: 93 points already. At three quarters it was 68–62, total 130; the last quarter was 31–19. Korea shot 50% from three and about 52% overall; Nigeria shot about 50% overall and 58.7% on twos. Jihyun Park scored 27. A full possession and shot-attempt reconstruction was not recovered, so a pace-versus-efficiency decomposition is not claimed. |
| Improvement | Injury attribution must be two-sided and role-specific. Jisu Park’s absence can remove Korean interior defence and alter Korea’s shot mix; it does not establish a 58–64 scoring ceiling or suppress Nigeria’s paint efficiency. Include Korean win/high-scoring and Korean win/low-scoring branches, with threes/attempt volume and opponent response recorded separately. |
| Grade | PROCESS_DEFECT — CROSS_ROW_COHERENCE / ATTRIBUTION_ASYMMETRY / SEPARATION_BUDGET. Exact hot-shooting magnitude is a realisation, not knowable beforehand. The omitted opponent-win score family was knowable from the frozen text. |

The March 77–60 Korea win was relevant evidence, but the changed interior personnel required decomposition rather than a wholesale inversion of winner and scoring floor. Nigeria’s preparation mean of 66.6 was also insufficient to estimate this matchup’s total without possessions, lineups and shooting opportunities. Nigeria reached 81 and still lost comfortably. Both teams contributing efficiently beat the Under; no overtime was required.

Over 143.5 and Korea +12.5 therefore won together. The winner annotation was not reconciled with the same underdog scoring branch that made Korea the second-ranked contract. Retain the identification of Korean perimeter upside, but operationalise it in the joint object instead of a sentence beside a Nigeria-only score tree. This reinforces L-033/L-037/L-043/L-059/L-060. It does not prove that the opposite total should always have been first or that future Korea games should automatically be Overs. [Official FIBA result, quarters and shooting fields](https://www.fiba.basketball/en/events/fiba-womens-basketball-world-cup-2026/games/128123-KOR-NGR).

### P-286 — deep #1 failure; correct six-over Under, wrong terminal resource model

| Question | Finding |
|---|---|
| What went right? | The card correctly separated the user’s six-over checkpoint from the possibly shortened regulatory powerplay. First-six Under 57.5 won at 54/2. Belfast were correctly named the winner. The identified boundary-hitting depth was useful to the winner call. |
| What went wrong? | #1 Under 120.5 lost at 146/3. The original argument said the Over usually needed a strong first six and little room for a two-over slowdown. Belfast won the early Under and still passed the full total comfortably. The printed joint examples included Under/Under and early Over/full Under, but omitted the important early Under/full Over branch. A full 20-over venue centre was also used as resistance to 10+ rpo in a compressed 12-over innings without a supported regime transformation. |
| Actual mechanism | Official scorer: 40/1 after the four-over regulatory powerplay, 54/2 after six, 63/3 after seven, then 75/3, 89/3, 113/3, 127/3 and 146/3. The final six contributed 92; the final three, 57. Chapman made 71* from 33 and Miller 23* from 11. Glasgow’s actual attack included Ngidi, Khan, van Meekeren, Neesham and Livingstone; Maharaj, cited as an important suppression resource, was absent. |
| Improvement | Model reduced innings as their own ball/resource/allocation regime. From a predeclared 54/2 six-over state, 67 more runs in 36 legal balls beats 120.5; that must be tested against the remaining hitters and actual bowling allocation. The official scorecard’s four-over powerplay and 12-over completion must be stored separately from the user’s six-over contract. |
| Grade | PROCESS_DEFECT — DEPENDENCE_TAIL / AVAILABILITY_EXPOSURE / STATE_FRESHNESS and evidence-cap inconsistency. CR-P4 said LOW, while the top row claimed LOW-MEDIUM. Final XI was openly unverified; Maharaj’s omission was not shown to be published before cutoff, so it is exposure uncertainty, not a proved ignored late announcement. |

Two factual corrections are needed. First, the source said secondary toss reporting had Belfast electing to bat; the official scorecard says **Glasgow won the toss and elected to bowl**. Belfast batting first was nevertheless the correct target. Second, NV Play’s ball-by-ball display can enumerate deliveries such as 4.7 or 9.8 when wides occur; those labels are delivery positions, not evidence of seven/eight legal balls in an over. The scorecard fall-of-wicket entry and completed-over boundaries control the legal-ball mapping.

The recorded pre-start rain delay lasted until 16:59 local; the card’s stated freeze was about 16:51, after the original 15:15 schedule. The user has now confirmed this non-live-labelled card was frozen pre-game, which is consistent with a freeze before actual play during a delay. Preserve the delay and stale-shell observation as state-quality facts, but do not use the scheduled-start crossing alone to exclude its pre-game historical results. Its final-XI/evidence-cap and phase-budget defects remain.

The Under loss repeats the resource-transition problem behind P-217/L-039, while the successful early Under demonstrates why phase success must not be discarded. The same available depth that supported Belfast winning also supported late acceleration. Do not blame Maxwell alone: he scored seven, while Chapman and Miller carried the late partnership. [Official match identity](https://www.etplofficial.com/matches/6a688c61b30844b0969df8af), [official-linked scorer](https://www.etplofficial.com/nvplay-embed?customerId=1A9E0000-8D41-7CED-D487-08DEF280CD56&innings=2&matchId=ac4fe6d2-e979-49db-9803-83495a8a047f&theme=light&widget=match-centre).

### P-287 — #1 and winner right; arithmetic and game-handicap logic still defective

| Question | Finding |
|---|---|
| What went right? | Over 40.5 won at 48, and Paul was the correct winner. Bublik’s serve/set resistance and Paul’s return/second-serve edge generated a five-set contest. The card correctly gave four/five-set states material support. |
| What went wrong? | Bublik +2.5 lost: Paul won 27 games to 21. A long match does not ensure a close aggregate game count. The source’s claim that the cushion covers all Bublik wins is false in tennis, and four of six representative game sums were wrong. A winning total cannot excuse those errors. |
| Actual mechanism | After three sets Bublik led two sets to one and 17–15 in games. Paul won the last two 6–1, 6–3, a 12–4 game swing, finishing +6. Official return-points won were Paul 37% versus Bublik 29%; second-serve points won 60% versus 50%; break conversion 6/8 versus 2/7. These align with the named return-pressure route. |
| Improvement | Sum games from each set separately, calculate signed game margin independently of set winner, and query every supplied row on each branch. Do not change the set-count mixture merely because a cushion is ranked first. Review late-set score concentration without attributing it automatically to fatigue, injury or motivation. |
| Grade | PROCESS_DEFECT — SOURCE_TRANSFORMATION / IDENTITY_CONTRACT in score arithmetic and handicap support, despite correct #1/winner. The source explicitly disclosed missing full L15/L20 hard-court windows; this also remains an evidence limitation. The score alone cannot establish fatigue. |

Frozen examples, recomputed without editing the original:

| Branch as printed | Printed total | Correct total | Signed game margin / actual implication |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Bublik 7–6, 6–4, 7–6 | 33 | **36** | Bublik +4; Under 40.5 and Bublik +2.5 |
| Bublik 7–6, 4–6, 7–6, 6–4 | 43 | **46** | Bublik +2; Over and Bublik +2.5 |
| Bublik 7–6, 4–6, 7–6, 4–6, 6–4 | 50 | **56** | Games tied 28–28; Over and Bublik +2.5 |
| Paul 6–4, 7–6, 6–4 | 33 | 33 | Paul +5; Under and Paul -2.5 |
| Paul 7–6, 6–4, 4–6, 6–4 | 43 | 43 | Paul +3; **Bublik +2.5 loses**, rather than an unspecified boundary |
| Paul 6–7, 7–6, 6–4, 4–6, 6–4 | 49 | **56** | Paul +2; Over and Bublik +2.5 |

A mathematical counterexample disproves the universal cushion claim: a player can win 0–6, 0–6, 7–6, 7–6, 7–6, taking three sets while losing games 21–30. Their +2.5 fails. Conversely, quick comfortable underdog wins can cover with low totals. RULES_TENNIS §9.6(7) itself contained the false union/“ordinarily long” instruction; v3.5 corrects the rule, not just this card. L-057/L-058/L-060 remain useful only when their branch geometry is computed correctly. [Official US Open score and statistics](https://www.usopen.org/en_US/scores/stats/1315.html).

### P-289 — both top picks lost; unsupported territory superiority and one-sided numerical branches

| Question | Finding |
|---|---|
| What went right? | The card noticed Sydney’s prior margins benefited from opposition inaccuracy and identified Pritchard/Fitzgerald/Gavalas contest work as the Bulldogs’ upset route. It also warned that wind can generate turnovers/repeat entries as well as reduce conversion. Those risks were relevant. |
| What went wrong? | Sydney -10.5 and Under 82.5 lost; Sydney’s winner annotation was wrong. The decisive evidence was raw three-game scoring-shot differential (+10 Sydney, -5 Bulldogs), with no worked opponent adjustment. Bulldogs’ sample included North Melbourne, while Sydney’s included Euro-Yroke. The numerical game tree had Sydney ahead in every example; Bulldogs superiority appeared only in a prose upset note. |
| Actual mechanism | Bulldogs led 20–6 after one and 22–18 at half. Sydney led 32–28 after three, but Bulldogs won the final quarter **32–1** to finish 60–33. Bulldogs won clearances 33–18. Emma McDonald kicked three last-quarter goals. Sydney’s Taylor Smith withdrew during the warm-up; exact publication time versus the cutoff was not recovered. |
| Improvement | Opponent-adjust the opportunity chain before treating three-game differential as territorial superiority. Separate goals+behinds from tracked attempts, inside-50 entries and chance quality. Require a numerical Bulldogs win/separation branch and a last-quarter allocation budget. Returning-player scenarios must include non-participation/minute limits until the final warm-up update is verified. |
| Grade | PROCESS_DEFECT — MATCHUP_CONTEXT / SEPARATION_BUDGET / CROSS_ROW_COHERENCE. Smith’s unavailability is a material changed premise, but pre-cutoff knowability is INCONCLUSIVE; do not claim the forecaster ignored a published withdrawal without a timestamp. |

The final scoring-shot count was Bulldogs 15, Sydney eight. Both converted a similar fraction into goals (9/15 and 5/8); the result was not chiefly explained by Sydney having worse final conversion. The clearer observed differences were opportunity volume, clearance control and late separation. Sydney’s recovery to a four-point lead after three shows the original Sydney path existed temporarily, but a full-game cover needed its continuation through the final quarter.

The Under was still alive at three-quarter time with 60 total points, then lost to a 33-point final quarter. A generic wind-efficiency adjustment did not protect it against one-sided repeat opportunities and late goals. This echoes the Geelong case’s volume-versus-conversion distinction and P-270’s numerical omission of an opponent-win branch. It also repeats the importance of L-038/L-042 availability exposure, L-043 four-family stress and L-058 phase separation. The winning Bulldogs cushion and Over were low in the order; merely listing them is not counted as success for the top picks. [Official AFLW final](https://www.afl.com.au/aflw/matches/8905), [official match report](https://www.afl.com.au/aflw/news/1601974/brilliant-western-bulldogs-hand-sydney-swans-first-loss-of-the-aflw-season).

## P-233–P-235 — older provisional score rows resolved; source-led retrospective corrections

Fresh Xinhua reporting and independently authored Titan Sports reporting corroborate all three regulation finals and half-time goal fields. CFA’s portal did not yield a current exact-match report. Under the existing original-report corroboration fallback, this is enough to settle ordinary score/half outcomes, but neither report owns complete corners. Upgrading goals does not upgrade corners, operator terms, xG, shots or lineup knowledge. [Xinhua](https://www.news.cn/sports/20260902/2f6c7e41328c4604b3525bf42ddd2ac5/c.html), [Titan Sports](https://www.ttplus.cn/publish/app/data/2026/09/01/626066/os_news.html).

| ID / frozen rank | Contract | Verified endpoint | Updated settlement |
|---|---|---|---|
| P-233-C01 / 1 | Beijing team Over 1.5 | Beijing 3 | WIN |
| P-233-C02 / 2 | First-half Over 0.5 | Half 1–0 | WIN |
| P-233-C03 / 3 | Match Over 2.5 | Four goals | WIN |
| P-233-C04 / 4 | BTTS No | Both scored | LOSS |
| P-233-C05 / 5 | Corners Over 8.5 | Prior specialist 13; no new owner | PROVISIONAL WIN |
| P-234-C01 / 1 | First-half Over 0.5 | Half 0–0 | LOSS |
| P-234-C02 / 2 | Dalian team Over 0.5 | Dalian 1 | WIN |
| P-234-C03 / 3 | Corners Over 8.5 | Prior specialist 10; no new owner | PROVISIONAL WIN |
| P-234-C04 / 4 | BTTS Yes | Dalian 1–0 Shenhua | LOSS |
| P-234-C05 / 5 | Match Over 2.5 | One goal | LOSS |
| P-235-C01 / 1 | First-half Over 0.5 | Half 0–2 | WIN |
| P-235-C02 / 2 | Port team Over 0.5 | Port 3 | WIN |
| P-235-C03 / 3 | BTTS Yes | Shandong 0–3 Port | LOSS |
| P-235-C04 / 4 | Match Over 2.5 | Three goals | WIN |
| P-235-C05 / 5 | Corners Over 8.5 | Prior specialist 14; no new owner | PROVISIONAL WIN |

| Event | What went right and why | What went wrong / actual mechanism | Knowability, prior lessons and improvement |
|---|---|---|---|
| P-233 | Beijing team scoring and early Over aligned with a 26th-minute opener; Beijing eventually scored three | Clean-sheet assumption failed at 48 minutes. The second Beijing goal did not arrive until 86 minutes after a keeper handling error; the third was in added time. A 3–1 final does not mean comfortable scoring dominance throughout | Keep early scoring distinct from clean-sheet and terminal goal volume (L-037/L-043). The late error itself was not predictable. Grade exact probability weighting INCONCLUSIVE; retain the earlier matchup-context defect without treating the result as vindication of every premise |
| P-234 — deep #1 follow-up | Dalian team Over 0.5 won via a second-half penalty; it was correctly a distinct target from the early Over | First-half Over lost at 0–0. Dalian’s keeper was sent off following an early incident; Shenhua later also went down to ten. This was not an uncontaminated example of ordinary cautious knockout football. Reports differ by a minute on the penalty goal (53/54), with no consequence for half or final settlement | A red card can increase chance quality for one side while changing the other’s exposure; it has no automatic total direction. The exact dismissals were not pregame knowledge. Preserve the existing process critique about opponent/phase modelling, remove the older single-match “CALIBRATION” diagnosis, and do not fit a blanket knockout Under rule from this outcome (L-043/L-047/L-048) |
| P-235 | Port scoring and early Over aligned with goals at 18 and 22 minutes; final Over also won | BTTS failed because Shandong never scored. Port’s attack working is not evidence Shandong would generate its own scoring event | Preserve the successful one-team/early read; separate opponent scoring from match total (L-037/L-043). Previous winner/matchup criticism remains; no new personnel explanation is fabricated without pre-cutoff source evidence |

The early-goal losses in P-234 and P-250 do not establish a universal negative scoring sign for all knockout games. RULES_SOCCER control 28 and L-064 previously described that sign as mandatory despite a candidate-only evidence basis. This audit narrows the active control to **competition/round/aggregate-state conditioning and comparison with league context**. Any signed centre shift must be supported by the relevant evidence; the size and direction are not fitted or assumed from these retrospective cases. Post-goal and red-card regime changes remain separate.

## General and sport-specific learning disposition

| Item | Decision | Exact prospective change | Evidence / limits |
|---|---|---|---|
| L-068 | PROMOTED_PROCESS — arithmetic and contract validity | Compute set/game/quarter/inning/phase sums and every contract’s signed margin from one event tree. Correct tennis game-handicap union logic and do not rerank by set-count folklore | P-287 mathematical counterexamples; repairs validity, no empirical accuracy claim |
| L-069 | PROMOTED_PROCESS — failed-gate propagation | A missing final-role/XI handshake stays missing at the ranking. Reconcile named exposure after updates; failed LOW caps cannot become LOW-MEDIUM in the final table. Store late-change publication times or label knowability unknown | P-280 definite known-at error; P-286/P-289 exposure uncertainty. Do not falsely turn their unverified timing into a proved omission |
| L-070 | PROMOTED_PROCESS — two-sided numerical branch coverage | Map both teams’ ordinary win/separation states and both early/full total split directions. A prose upset sentence does not substitute for contract-evaluable branch arithmetic | P-285/P-289 one-sided examples; P-286 omitted early Under/full Over. Extends L-033/L-039/L-043/L-057, no assigned branch weights |
| L-071 | PROMOTED_PROCESS — source identity and field semantics | Keep host+event ID+date+participants together; distinguish legal balls from delivery sequence labels, scoring shots from attempts, points from efficiency, and final headers from placeholders | AFL host ID collisions, NV Play numbering, FIBA efficiency, NRL headline discrepancy; source-transformation repair |
| L-064 / soccer control 28 | NARROWED PROCESS; automatic negative sign retired | Require exact knockout/aggregate regime data and a sourced direction; no blanket downward centre shift | No validated signed adjustment exists; P-234 had dismissals and is not a clean baseline case |
| C-P293-BB-CLUSTERS | CANDIDATE; zero completions | Compare symmetric contact/defence and inherited-runner stress representation against existing baseball process | P-281/P-282 are origins, not two prospective completions |
| C-P293-CR-RESOURCES | CANDIDATE; zero completions | Compare resource-conditioned remaining-run distributions against aggregate venue/phase summaries, separately for T20I and reduced ETPL | P-283/P-286; no pooling of 12-over and 20-over scoring centres |
| C-P293-BK-ALLOCATION | CANDIDATE; zero completions | Compare opponent-adjusted possessions/shot mix plus two-team bench phases against raw warm-up and ranking narratives | P-284/P-285; possessions not reconstructed here, therefore no fitted pace explanation |
| C-P293-AFL-VOLUME | CANDIDATE; zero completions | Compare opponent-adjusted opportunities plus conversion/wind interactions against raw three-game shot differential | Geelong/P-289; AFL and AFLW remain separate populations |
| C-P293-NRL-ROLE | CANDIDATE; zero completions | Compare timestamped expected playing roles/minutes against nominal team-sheet positions | P-280; validity correction immediate, predictive contribution still unmeasured |
| C-P293-TEN-ALLOCATION | CANDIDATE; zero completions | Compare joint set/game allocation with a set-count-only or close-match narrative | P-287; arithmetic correction immediate, no automatic fatigue sign |

Candidate implementation requires a frozen population, champion/challenger, independent event unit, primary metric and guardrails, review horizon, uncertainty procedure and falsification rule **before** any prospective cases count. The ten completed user-confirmed pre-game cards count in their issued v3.4 historical ranking cohort. They are origin/development cases for the changes designed after their outcomes and add no new-method test completions. The P-275 recovery-length test has no completed frozen comparator implementation; eligibility confirmation alone cannot invent one. Numerical-training counts remain unchanged.

The user’s objective that the top two should ideally win is retained as a diagnostic. With this cohort, improving that objective requires better preferred-side selection, not counting complementary rows as independent successes. Publication of probabilities, ROI, stakes or a claimed improved hit rate remains unsupported.

## Component import — P-294–P-305 second continuation — 2026-09-05(b)

A second local mini-log continuation (`P-294`–`P-305`, twelve fresh pregame cards) plus the five cards the September 5(a) audit had deferred live at first check (`P-288`, `P-290`, `P-291`, `P-292`, `P-293`) were supplied to this session for settlement, retrospective and algorithm-update work. Unlike the `P-272`–`P-279` and `P-280`–`P-293` imports, this component was supplied inline in the conversation rather than as a discoverable upstream file with an independent byte/hash trail, so no pre-session git-history or filesystem-timestamp fingerprint can be claimed for it — that limitation is recorded honestly rather than fabricating a fingerprint. The full per-card text (frozen identity/contract tables, precondition audits, team/availability audits, scenario/kill-path maps, corridor budgets, frozen rankings, rank-1 coherence checks, potential-winner sections and complete source registers for all twelve cards) is preserved in this session's own record and archived at:
