| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Issued rank | 1. Under 56.5 (LEAN, MEDIUM) 2. Cowboys -5.5 (LEAN, MEDIUM-LOW) 3. Raiders +5.5 (FORCED RANK) 4. Over 56.5 (FORCED RANK) |
| Actual result | Raiders 50, Cowboys 30. Raiders led 30-12 at halftime. Debutant Canberra half Coby Black scored a **premiership-record 26 points on debut** (2 tries, 9/9 goals) — the most in 118 years of the competition by a debutant. Cowboys co-captain Reuben Cotter was sin-binned and placed on report 15 seconds into the match. |
| What went right | The card's own pregame research explicitly surfaced the mechanism that beat it: Coby Black's NSW Cup profile (77 goals at 77%, 17 try assists, 362.8 average kicking metres across 19 appearances) was flagged as "capable rather than replacement-level," and Canberra's use of the fixture "to expose younger players to NRL intensity" was named as the winner kill path for the Cowboys. The season-scoring-environment total read (both teams around 48-49 combined per season baseline) was directionally reasonable in isolation — Canberra's own recent 10-game total sample showed 7/10 Under 56.5 — and Cowboys' spine-continuity advantage over a debuting halfback is an ordinarily sound rugby-league heuristic. |
| What went wrong | The named kill path was written into the evidence table but not carried into the ranking weight. `Under 56.5` and `Cowboys -5.5` were still placed at #1 and #2 despite the card's own text calling Black "capable" with legitimate underlying production, and despite naming a credible Canberra +5.5/upset branch (`RL-B5`) — that branch was correctly *identified* but ranked #3, below two rows it was already flagged as capable of defeating. The Cotter sin-bin 15 seconds in (a live, unknowable-pregame event) then removed Cowboys' primary go-forward middle forward for ten minutes and compounded an already-live upset. |
| Knowability before issue | The debutant-quality signal (NSW Cup rate stats) was fully knowable and was in fact recorded pregame. The sin-bin timing was not knowable pregame and is not counted against the forecast. |
| Process grade | **PROCESS_DEFECT — RANK_WEIGHT_INCONSISTENT_WITH_NAMED_EVIDENCE.** This is the same failure class as L-033 ("when a material adverse branch is supported enough to be named, explain why it remains subordinate") — no such explicit subordination reasoning was given for Black's kicking/creation numbers relative to "replacement-level debutant" framing used to keep Raiders +5.5 at #3. |
| Improvement adopted | **L-075** (new, see LEARNING_REGISTER.md): when a reserve-grade/debutant player's own underlying per-appearance production (goal-kicking %, assist rate, running/kicking metres) is retrieved and exceeds what a generic "replacement-level" framing would imply, that production must be explicitly propagated into the exposure chain and the ranking weight, with a written sentence reconciling why it is or is not discounted — not left in a risk paragraph while the ranking proceeds as if it were absent. RULES_NRL_RUGBY.md §8 (kill-path library, ordering overrides) updated accordingly. |

#### `P-299` — Mali (W) vs Spain (W), FIBA Women's World Cup

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Issued rank | 1. Spain -25.5 (LEAN, MEDIUM) 2. Under 144.5 (LEAN/FORCED RANK, MEDIUM-LOW) 3. Mali +25.5 (FORCED RANK) 4. Over 144.5 (FORCED RANK) |
| Actual result | **Mali 82, Spain 73** — one of the biggest upsets of the tournament's opening round. Mali outscored Spain 31-19 in the third quarter on a 12-2 run and controlled the lead through the fourth. Djénéba N'Diaye had 19 points for Mali; Iyana Martín had 20 for Spain. |
| What went right | The card correctly discounted Spain's Aug 29 friendly win (97-58, a 39-point margin) as unrepresentative, explicitly noting Spain were missing five eventual World Cup rotation players (Fam, Conde, Carrera, Flórez, Gustafson) in that friendly, and separately noting **Sika Koné did not appear in Mali's reported box score for that friendly at all**. The card also correctly named the exact kill path that materialised: "Mali's elite offensive rebounding and Koné/Sangaré/N'Diaye interior/creation keep Spain from finishing defensive possessions and compress the margin." |
| What went wrong | Despite naming Koné's absence from the only head-to-head data point available and despite naming the precise mechanism that produced the actual result, the card still anchored its corridor ("Spain 84-90 central, Mali 56-64 central") close enough to the discounted friendly's implied gap that Spain -25.5 was ranked #1. A friendly with a confirmed-missing best player on the losing side is not partial evidence to be modestly discounted — it is close to zero evidence for margin-setting purposes once that player returns, and should have been used for style/matchup notes only. Mali's Group A opener (a 97-102 near-upset of Japan, requiring Japan to hit a Women's World Cup record 20 threes to survive) was in the record and was, if anything, a stronger and more current signal of Mali's real ceiling than the discounted friendly, but it did not move Spain off Rank #1. |
| Knowability before issue | Koné's absence from the friendly and Mali's competitive Japan game were both fully known and cited pregame. The magnitude of Mali's Q3 run was not knowable, but the qualitative existence of a live upset mechanism was already on the record. |
| Process grade | **PROCESS_DEFECT — REFERENCE_BAND_ANCHORED_TO_DISCOUNTED_EVIDENCE.** The card performed the discount qualitatively (in prose) but did not propagate it quantitatively into the corridor/rank. |
| Improvement adopted | **L-076** (new): an exhibition/preparation-game margin must be explicitly downgraded toward the low end of its evidence band — and excluded from anchoring the reference corridor — whenever either side's core rotation (a name-level absence, not a general "some players missing" note) is confirmed absent from that exhibition. Any other same-tournament data point (even against a different opponent) for the affected team's *current* full roster should be weighted above a stale friendly result once such a friendly's core-absence caveat is recorded. RULES_BASKETBALL.md §8 updated with this branch and a new named kill path. |

### What went right — clean top-two wins (`P-296`, `P-303`)

| Card | Why the read matched the outcome |
|---|---|
| `P-296` Doosan @ SSG (KBO) | Both starters (Choi Min-seok, Kim Min-jun) were correctly identified as legitimate, currently in-form arms with low walk/home-run rates; the card explicitly refused to let Doosan's 24-inning scoreless drought become a deterministic continuation rule (correct application of L-011/L-062 — a streak was named, its baseline checked, and no automatic-continuation weight was assigned), while still keeping SSG's fresher bullpen (Avila's complete-game shutout the day before) as a live, correctly-weighted advantage. Result: 3-2, a clean low-scoring, close SSG win exactly inside the card's own "central total 5-9" corridor. |
| `P-303` Nigeria (W) vs Hungary (W) (FIBA WWC) | The card used the conservative, cushion-based framing (Nigeria +25.5, i.e. backing the stronger/higher-ranked side's floor rather than an outright large-spread cover) instead of the aggressive framing used unsuccessfully in `P-299`. It weighted Nigeria's higher FIBA world ranking (No. 8 vs No. 19), Nigeria's opening-game individual production (Okonkwo 22/7/3) and Hungary's poor shooting in a blowout loss to France (32% overall) without discounting Hungary's one credible counter-path (Dorka Juhász, who did return and did post a 10/10 line) to zero — it simply outranked it correctly. **Corrected 2026-09-06(d) (second independent review, finding F21):** the actual final, 81-53 (total 134, margin 28), lands **outside** the card's own stated "Nigeria 72-78, Hungary 64-71, combined 136-149" corridor on every one of its four stated bounds (Nigeria 81>78; Hungary 53<64; total 134<136; margin 28 far exceeds the corridor's implied 1-14 range). The original text here claimed it landed "almost exactly" inside that corridor, which is not correct on any checked bound. The card's winner call, ranking rationale and Rank #1/#2 contract outcomes remain correct and are unaffected by this correction — a winning contract does not retroactively make an inaccurate corridor accurate, and this framework's own rules already require winning cards to receive the same arithmetic audit as losing ones (`L-115`). |

The contrast between `P-299` (aggressive favourite-spread framing, failed) and `P-303` (conservative underdog-cushion framing on the stronger side, succeeded) issued on the same day at the same tournament is itself informative: see the new `C-PL15-BSK-CUSHION-VS-SPREAD` candidate added to LEARNING_REGISTER.md, which also folds in `P-293`'s Gold Coast +13.5 cushion win — three large-cushion "team getting points" rows this session, three covers.

### Validation questions — answered across this cohort

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| Were confirmed starting/bench lineups and coaching information obtained for both teams, every game? | **Partially.** Official 24-hour or team-list confirmation was obtained pregame for `P-294`, `P-295`, `P-298`, `P-300`, `P-301`, `P-302` (with named absences and role changes). `P-296`/`P-297` (KBO) had confirmed **starting pitchers** but explicitly could not obtain independently-verified same-day final batting orders before the freeze in either card — both cards recorded this as a `PARTIAL` gate rather than silently assuming lineups, which is the correct discipline, but it remains a real, recurring gap for same-day KBO lineups specifically (see RULES_BASEBALL.md addition). `P-299`/`P-303` (FIBA) had full roster confirmation but not independently-verified same-day starting fives — again correctly flagged `PARTIAL` rather than assumed. Coaching/tactical-system information was present qualitatively (e.g. Newcastle Jaissle's team news, Canterbury's ruck/line-speed issues) but not as a dedicated structured field in any card; this is a documentation-format gap worth closing (see RULES_GENERAL.md addition below). |
| Were the sources used accurate, or did they need newer/more accurate replacements? | **Mixed**, and this pass surfaced two concrete, fixable defects rather than a general accuracy problem. (1) `nrl.com`'s own match-centre pages now sit behind a login wall for automated fetches (redirect to `account.nrl.com/authorize`) — the ABC News NRL/NRLW Score Centre (`abc.net.au/news/sport/score-centre/...`) was used successfully as a reliable, login-free fallback for both `P-294` and `P-295`'s final scores and is recorded as the preferred automated-fetch source for NRL/NRLW final scores going forward. (2) `etplofficial.com`'s "matches" listing page produces AI-summarised fetch results that can be internally self-contradictory (see L-074) — a literal/verbatim re-fetch resolved it correctly. Every other final score in this pass (KBO, FIBA, AFLW, EPL, MLB, Liga MX, tennis) was corroborated from at least one clearly-labelled official or major-outlet source, and the two lowest-confidence figures (`P-300` powerplay, `P-302` corners) were correctly left unresolved rather than asserted from a single ambiguous source. |
| Were there blind spots in the pre-game analysis, and how should the next card account for them? | **Yes, three recurring ones**, each now covered by a specific rule (L-073 through L-077, detailed above/below): (1) named-but-subordinate kill paths not propagating into the final rank weight (`P-295`, `P-299` — L-033's existing control was insufficiently specific about *how* to propagate, now sharpened by L-075/L-076); (2) a systematic tendency to rank a season-average-derived Under total at #1 ahead of a spread/moneyline row that better captured the specific game's blowout-or-competitive mechanism (`P-294`, `P-298`, `P-301`, `P-297` reversed — tracked under `C-PL11-ALL-TOTAL-DIRECTION`, now with six more data points); (3) corners and (for ETPL) powerplay-level cricket splits remain the two most structurally under-sourced market types in this framework's entire history — 10+ consecutive corners rows and now two consecutive ETPL powerplay rows have gone unresolved. L-073 responds to (3) directly by capping corners out of Rank #1 whenever an alternative total exists in the same slate. |

### Source register additions — 2026-09-05(b)

| Source | Role / limitation | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|
| ABC News NRL/NRLW Score Centre (`abc.net.au/news/sport/score-centre/nrl(w)/<date>/<slug>/<id>`) | Reliable, login-free automated-fetch final score for NRL and NRLW; verbatim-quote fetch confirmed self-consistent on two separate cards this pass | **Promote to preferred automated NRL/NRLW final-score source**, ahead of `nrl.com` match centre for automated (non-browser) fetches |
| `nrl.com/draw/.../match-centre` | Now redirects automated fetches to an NRL account login wall (`account.nrl.com/authorize`) | Keep as a browser-accessible source only; do not rely on it for automated WebFetch retrieval going forward |
| `etplofficial.com/matches` (listing page) | AI-summarised fetches of this specific page produced one internally self-contradictory result this pass (batting-order claim contradicted its own stated win-margin type); a literal/verbatim quote request on the same URL resolved correctly | Always request a verbatim/literal quote from this URL, never an interpreted summary; treat a first summarised read as provisional until re-confirmed verbatim |
| Czech live-blog sources (`isport.blesk.cz`, `livesport.cz`) for Chance Liga | Both returned early-match cached snapshots (0-0 at 6' and 0-0 at 25') for the same fixture on two separate fetches — consistent with each other and with the fixture's known kickoff time, correctly indicating a genuinely live match rather than stale/conflicting data | Acceptable live-state corroboration source for Czech football when two independent fetches agree on elapsed time direction |
| MyKBO Stats (`mykbostats.com`) individual game pages | Returned HTTP 403 to automated fetch this pass | Do not rely on for automated retrieval; Sofascore and Korean-language news sources remain the working KBO same-day lanes per L-067 |

## Complete September 5 change and instruction record

All audit decisions, adopted controls, the user-confirmed pre-game eligibility correction, sport-rule implementation, source updates and validation are consolidated in [AUDIT_CHANGELOG_2026-09-05.md](AUDIT_CHANGELOG_2026-09-05.md). The [full retrospective](COMPREHENSIVE_SETTLEMENT_AUDIT_2026-09-05.md), [status list](GAME_LOG_STATUS_INDEX_2026-09-05.md) and [eligibility register](PREGAME_ELIGIBILITY_REGISTER_2026-09-05.md) are authoritative Markdown records. **Standing user instruction:** all audit changes, updates, learnings, rule changes, instructions and logs must be recorded in the Markdown documents. The `.md` set is the complete human-readable authority. Scripts, JSON and CSV are validation/data companions, not the only record of a decision.

For each future change, update the active prediction log, the specific sport rule document or RULES_GENERAL for cross-sport controls, LEARNING_REGISTER for disposition, and relevant method/source/workflow documents in the same pass. Append a dated, source-linked retrospective with original ranks, results, what went right/wrong, knowability, prior lessons and the exact adopted change. Record pending fields and live-at-first-check deferrals in the active queue. Preserve original issued cards and label superseding corrections. Document validation and link the changed Markdown files in the audit change log before delivery.

## 2026-09-06 — settlement, evidence-gap closure and the v3.7 algorithm patch

Method at settlement: `MDS-2026.09.06-v3.7 — SPORTS_ONLY / MARKET_BLIND`. Probability state `NOT_GENERATED / NOT_PUBLISHED`. No forecast was issued in this pass; it is a settlement, retrospective and rule-repair pass. Archived component: [`archive/PREDICTION_MINI_LOG_12_P304_P305_SETTLED_2026-09-06.md`](archive/PREDICTION_MINI_LOG_12_P304_P305_SETTLED_2026-09-06.md). Full narrative: [`IMPROVEMENT_PLAN_2026-09-06.md`](IMPROVEMENT_PLAN_2026-09-06.md).

### Settlement evidence

**`P-304`.** [ČTK / ceskenoviny.cz](https://www.ceskenoviny.cz/zpravy/slavia-v-primem-souboji-o-prvni-misto-v-lize-rozdrtila-zbrojovku/2870539) supplied full-time score, half-time score, all four scorers with minutes, both starting XIs, substitution minutes, both head coaches (Trpišovský, Svědík) and the referee (Jan Beneš) in one fetch. Corroborated by the progressive headline sequence on [the iSport.cz live-blog article URL](https://isport.blesk.cz/clanek/fotbal-chance-liga/479501/online-slavia-zbrojovka-4-0-debakl-ctvrtou-branku-pridal-jurasek-dve-zraneni-opor.html) — `0:0` → `1:0. Chorý proměnil penaltu!` → `3:0` → `4:0` — which independently establishes that the only first-half goal was the 38th-minute penalty. Both sources native-language, satisfying the `v3.4` requirement.

**`P-305` and `P-300`.** `site.api.espn.com/apis/site/v2/sports/cricket/1547871/summary?event=<id>`. The `notes[]` array carries per-innings `Powerplay 1: Overs 0.1 - 6.0 (Mandatory - N runs, W wickets)` and a `toss` note. Values: event `1547886` — `60 runs, 1 wicket` (Amsterdam), `69 runs, 1 wicket` (Dublin), toss `"Dublin Guardians , elected to field first"`. Event `1547885` — `28 runs, 3 wickets` (ECR), `37 runs, 4 wickets` (Rotterdam), toss `"Rotterdam Dockers , elected to field first"`. Cross-checked against the ESPN cricket scoreboard endpoint and the ESPNcricinfo full scorecard: ECR's fall of wickets has three down by 2.6 overs and the fourth at 8.4, and Amsterdam's second wicket falls at 7.4 — both arithmetically consistent with the powerplay notes.

**`P-302`, `P-273`, `P-151`.** `site.api.espn.com/apis/site/v2/sports/soccer/<league>/summary?event=<id>`, field `boxscore.teams[].statistics.wonCorners`. Events `401879286` (`eng.1`), `401911809` (`ita.coppa_italia`), `401841527` (`arg.1`). The `P-151` figure of 11–3 exactly reproduces the previously specialist-only value, which is what upgrades it from provisional to confirmed.

### Deep retrospective

#### `P-305` Rank #1 WIN — was the edge real, or a coin-flip that landed?

Answered from data rather than assertion. Every completed **full six-over** powerplay in ETPL 2026 before these two matches, retrieved from the same new lane:

| Innings | PP score |
|---|---|
| Dublin Guardians (M9) | 49/2 |
| Edinburgh Castle Rockers (M9) | 68/3 |
| Glasgow Cosmic (M10) | 39/2 |
| Rotterdam Dockers (M10) | 28/1 |
| Dublin Guardians (M11) | 50/1 |
| Rotterdam Dockers (M11) | 61/1 |
| Edinburgh Castle Rockers (M12) | 55/1 |
| Belfast Wolves (M12) | 51/4 |
| Glasgow Cosmic (M13) | 56/0 |
| **Amsterdam Flames (M13)** | **72/1** |

**CORRECTED 2026-09-06(d)** (second independent review, finding F15): this fixture is officially ESPN's **12th Match**, not the 13th. The actual 13th Match is Glasgow Cosmic v Belfast Wolves (reduced to 12 overs, already recorded correctly in `P-286`) and is a different physical fixture, excluded from this population because its powerplay was reduced, not full-length. Full correction and the open `P-099`/`P-115`/`P-171` reconciliation gap: [`IMPROVEMENT_PLAN_2026-09-06.md`](IMPROVEMENT_PLAN_2026-09-06.md).

Sorted: 28, 39, 49, 50, 51, 55, 56, 61, 68, 72. Median 53, mean 52.9.

At `P-305`'s line of 51.5 the **competition-level Over rate is 5/10 — a pure coin-flip**. But Amsterdam's own single prior full powerplay, 72/1, was the highest figure in the competition. That is event-specific, same-competition, same-personnel evidence of exactly the kind `G26` requires for a Rank #1, and the row won for the stated reason. **Verdict: result-right / process-right.**

#### `P-300` Rank #1 LOSS — mandatory deep audit

At `P-300`'s line of 50.5 the competition-level Over rate was **6/10**, and ECR's own two prior powerplays (68/3, 55/1) were both Overs. On runs alone that is a *better*-supported row than `P-305`'s. It lost at 28/3. The explanation is in the column the card dropped.

ECR's supportive powerplays were **68 for three** and **55 for one** — they scored quickly *while losing wickets*. A top three of high-strike-rate openers (Smuts, Gous, Adair) does not produce a symmetric powerplay distribution; it produces a **bimodal** one, a flying-start mode and a collapse mode, with little mass between them. The mean of that distribution — a healthy ~61 for ECR — is a value the side rarely actually posts. In Match 14 the collapse mode arrived: three wickets inside 2.6 overs (Smuts 1.2, Gous 1.6, Adair 2.6), powerplay 28.

The venue is not the explanation. The very next match on the same ground produced powerplays of 60 and 69.

| `G38` field | Finding |
|---|---|
| Identity / contract | **No defect.** The conditional structure was correctly stated and the condition was met |
| Source transformation | **No defect at issue**, but a **retrieval-completeness defect**: phase runs retrieved without phase wickets, discarding the variable that separates the two modes |
| Arithmetic | No defect |
| Temporal leakage | No defect |
| Settlement | **Defect at the previous pass** — declared an evidence gap when a structured source existed. Corrected here |
| Compliance | No defect |
| Process lock | **Not applied.** A retrieval gap is repaired by a new required field, not by a quarantine |
| **Verdict** | **result-wrong / process-broadly-right, one named repairable retrieval gap** |

Cost of the ordering: the same card's Rank #2 (`20-over Under 168.5`) won by 20.5 runs. Under the new `G26.1` separation floor, a row on a 60% competition split with a bimodal underlying distribution cannot take the top slot from a row with wider separation. That reordering alone converts this card from a Rank #1 loss to a Rank #1 win. Adopted as `L-083` and written into `RULES_CRICKET.md` §September 6.

#### `P-302` Rank #1 WIN — the row was gradable all along

Bournemouth's 3 corners came from a low-possession, high-volume shooting profile (17 shots, 7 blocked, 42.7% possession), not from territorial pressure — a distinction now required in `RULES_SOCCER.md` §8.10, because a possession-based justification for a corners row is `align = PROXY` under `G23`.

The consequential part is not the win. On 2026-09-05 this row was written off as unresolvable and became the tenth data point promoting `L-073`, a blanket rule that corners may never be Rank #1. **The number was in a keyless public API, one request away.** A promoted control resting on a false empirical premise is worse than a lost pick, because it silently degrades every future slate containing that row type and it looks like accumulated experience while doing so. Corrected as `L-081`, with a standing requirement that any future "this has never worked" promotion must state the search that was run and the endpoints tried.

#### `P-304` Rank #1 WIN — and the blindspot it exposed

`1st Half Over 0.5` is a *union* contract: it wins if any one goal lands in a ~48-minute window. It converted in the 38th minute — late in the window, which is itself informative. The margin of safety came from the number of paths, not from confidence in any one of them. This is the second consecutive settlement of this contract type in two days (`P-302`'s `1H Over 0.5` also won), and it is the basis of the `G21.1` path-geometry disclosure.

The blindspot: **two of Slavia's four goals came from substitutes** — Ayaosi (76'), on as a *forced injury replacement* in the 26th minute, and Jurásek (88'), on at 63' — after two starters were lost to injury inside the first half hour. No rule in this framework required a card to record who was on the bench. Had the frozen slate carried a full-match total or a margin row, bench quality would have been the single largest unmodelled driver. Adopted as `L-082` / `G14.2`.

Favourably, the same card is a **third instance of `L-077`**: Slavia lost two starters early and still won 4-0, because the opponent was a promoted side. A flat personnel-loss markdown cannot represent that; an opponent-conditioned one can.

### Over/Under diagnosis — the objective of at least one winning total row

Across the twelve new cards `P-294`–`P-305`, counting every ranked total-type row (full-game, half and phase totals):

| Measure | Result |
|---|---|
| Cards where at least one ranked O/U row won | **8 of 12** — `P-296`, `P-298`, `P-300`, `P-301`, `P-302`, `P-303`, `P-304`, `P-305` |
| Cards where every ranked O/U row lost | **4 of 12** — `P-294`, `P-295`, `P-297`, `P-299` |
| Ranked **Over** rows | **4 W / 1 L** |
| Ranked **Under** rows | **5 W / 4 L** |
| Direction of every failure | **All four failing cards failed on an `Under`** |

The September 5(a) cohort recorded "preferred O/U 3/10 events." 8/12 is a real improvement on that, reported as a descriptive count and not as a hit rate.

**CORRECTED 2026-09-06(d) — this table is wrong; see the full correction below.** A second independent review found that `P-294`, `P-295`, `P-297` and `P-299` — the four cards listed here as having *no* winning O/U row — each already carry a **winning Rank #4 Over row** in this same settlement table (`P-294` Over 48.5 WIN, `P-295` Over 56.5 WIN, `P-297` Over 11.0 WIN, `P-299` Over 144.5 WIN). "At least one ranked O/U row won" is therefore close to **12 of 12**, not 8 of 12 — almost mechanically so, because Over and Under are usually supplied as an exact complementary pair (`L-055`), so if the ranked Under loses, its complementary Over is very likely to win. This was already the framework's own stated caution and this table violated it. The honest, non-mechanical measure is **highest-ranked O/U selection accuracy: 7 of 12**. Full corrected breakdown, verified against the settlement rows above: **[2026-09-06(d) correction](#2026-09-06d--second-independent-review-corrections)**.

In all four failures the *winning* side scored more than the central estimate the card used. The cards budgeted both sides centrally and never wrote down the total implied by an output the favourite had **already produced** inside the retrieved window. `P-299` is the sharpest case: Spain's plausible upper output plus Mali's median lands essentially on the 155 that actually occurred, against a line of 144.5.

Three fixes, all of which improve *which* total row takes the top slot rather than tilting direction:

1. **`G20.2` aggregate tail budget** — print `T_hi_fav`, `T_hi_dog`, `T_hi_both` against every `Under` line from the L10 data already retrieved at `G13.1`; symmetric lower-tail sums for `Over` rows. A `TAIL_EXPOSED` row needs one sentence naming the mechanism that stops an already-observed combination recurring, or it forfeits Rank #1. **Honest limitation: this is a humility gate, not a predictor** — on this cohort it would also have flagged `P-296`, which won. On `P-296` the sentence was easy and evidenced; on `P-294` it could not have been written honestly. That difference is the point.
2. **`G21.1` path geometry** — classify every total row `UNION_LOW_THRESHOLD` / `INTERSECTION_CONSTRAINT` / `CENTRAL_BAND`, printing `N` for union rows. A central-band row may not outrank a union row from the same event without named event-specific evidence.
3. **`G26.1` top-slot separation floor** — a row on a 40–60% reference split takes Rank #1 only with named event-specific evidence moving it outside the band.

**Explicitly preserved:** the existing instruction not to promote an opposite pick solely to manufacture an O/U win. None of the three fixes creates a direction preference, and `G23.1`'s prohibition on mechanical reference-band moves is unchanged.

### Updated descriptive ledger

Cohort `P-288`, `P-290`–`P-305` (17 events):

| Measure | Value |
|---|---|
| Rank #1 resolvable rows | 17 |
| **Rank #1** | **9 WIN / 8 LOSS** |
| Rank #1 WINs | `P-290`, `P-291`, `P-293`, `P-296`, `P-297`, `P-302`, `P-303`, `P-304`, `P-305` |
| Rank #1 LOSSes | `P-288`, `P-292`, `P-294`, `P-295`, `P-298`, `P-299`, `P-300`, `P-301` |
| **Rank #2** (distinct #2 on record; `P-304`/`P-305` excluded — archival omission) | **7 WIN / 3 LOSS** |
| Both top two correct | 3 of 10 (`P-296`, `P-302`, `P-303`) |
| Both top two wrong | 2 of 10 (`P-295`, `P-299`) |

Forced complementary pairs mean raw row counts overstate information content (`L-055`). **This is a descriptive ledger. It is not calibration, edge, ROI or model validation, and it promotes no rule to a forecast weight.**

**CORRECTED 2026-09-06(d) — this table is incomplete, not just imprecise.** `P-288`, `P-290`, `P-291`, `P-292` and `P-293` do **not** lack a Rank #2 — their full ranked slates were preserved earlier in this same file (see the raw component import sections) and were simply not carried into this summary table. Restoring them gives a **matched 15-card cohort** (not 17 for Rank #1 alone, not 10 for Rank #2 alone): **Rank #1 7 WIN / 8 LOSS (46.7%)**, **Rank #2 10 WIN / 5 LOSS (66.7%)**, both top two correct **5 of 15**, both wrong **3 of 15**. Adding the two ungradable cards (`P-304`, `P-305`, Rank #1 only) gives the correct **17-card Rank #1 figure of 9 WIN / 8 LOSS**, which happens to be unchanged by this correction — only the Rank #2 and joint figures were wrong. Full detail: **[2026-09-06(d) correction](#2026-09-06d--second-independent-review-corrections)**.

### Validation questions, answered

**Confirmed starting and bench lineups, including coaching?** For `P-304`: both XIs, the substitutes who appeared, both head coaches and the referee — yes. The full unused bench — no. For `P-305`: both playing elevens — yes; cricket has no bench in the substitution sense; coaches — not retrieved. **But the honest finding is that the framework never required any of it at forecast time**, and the archived cards do not record whether it was captured. `G14.2` now requires it on every card, with `BENCH_NOT_RETRIEVED` blocking a margin or full-game total row from Rank #1.

**Were the sources accurate?** Two were dangerous — a fabricated "AI Simulation" article that would have recorded the wrong winner (`L-079`), and a search summary that repeated its scoreline as fact. `api.sofascore.com` is now `403` on every route tested and is downgraded for programmatic use. Two are materially better than anything previously in the register: the ESPN structured endpoints, and native-language Czech reporting.

**Blindspots?** Five, each with an implemented gate rather than an intention: bench/coaching capacity (`G14.2`); phase totals modelled on runs without possession-loss (`L-083`); no settlement-source pre-registration (`G10.2`); Under rows budgeted without the already-observed upper tail (`G20.2`); and a near-50/50 row able to claim the top slot unchallenged (`G26.1`).

## 2026-09-06(d) — second independent review: verification, corrections, and self-consistency repair

**Trigger:** the user asked for a second, separately supplied document (`GAME_LOG_BLINDSPOT_REVIEW_2026-09-06.md`, found already present at the repository root) to be fully reviewed and merged. Every one of its 30 findings (`F01`–`F30`) was checked against primary source before any correction was made — the settlement table on this page, the ESPN API records retrieved earlier this session, and the raw preserved component text further up this file. Full disposition: `LEARNING_REGISTER.md` §"2026-09-06(d) second independent review disposition." Received document preserved: [`archive/GAME_LOG_BLINDSPOT_REVIEW_2026-09-06_RECEIVED.md`](archive/GAME_LOG_BLINDSPOT_REVIEW_2026-09-06_RECEIVED.md).

**Headline, stated plainly: this review found real, confirmable errors in this session's own immediately preceding work — not only in older, inherited material.** Several of the corrections below are to text this same session wrote on 2026-09-06 earlier the same day. That is recorded honestly rather than minimised.

### 1. The O/U diagnosis was wrong — corrected

The earlier claim ("at least one ranked O/U row won in 8 of 12... every failing card failed on an Under") is **contradicted by this page's own settlement table**: `P-294`, `P-295`, `P-297` and `P-299` — the four cards listed as total failures — each already carry a **winning Rank #4 Over row** (`P-294` Over 48.5 WIN, `P-295` Over 56.5 WIN, `P-297` Over 11.0 WIN, `P-299` Over 144.5 WIN). Recomputed honestly, from every ranked total-type row across the twelve `P-294`–`P-305` cards, excluding corners:

| Card | Over result(s) | Under result(s) | Highest-ranked O/U |
|---|---|---|---|
| P-294 | W | L | L |
| P-295 | W | L | L |
| P-296 | L | W | W |
| P-297 | W | L | L |
| P-298 | L | W | W |
| P-299 | W | L | L |
| P-300 | PP L; innings L | innings W; PP W | L |
| P-301 | L | W | W |
| P-302 | 1H W; FT W | FT L; 1H L | W |
| P-303 | L | W | W |
| P-304 | 1H W | not preserved | W |
| P-305 | PP W (cond. met) | not preserved | W |
| **Total** | **8 W / 6 L** | **6 W / 6 L** | **7 W / 5 L** |

**Corrected conclusion:** across 26 available total rows, **14 W / 12 L**. Because Over and Under are usually supplied as an exact complementary pair (`L-055`), "at least one ranked O/U row won" is close to **12 of 12** — near-mechanical, not an 8-of-12 finding, and not informative on its own. The honest, non-mechanical measure is **highest-ranked O/U selection accuracy: 7 of 12 (58%)**. This replaces every "8 of 12" / "Overs 4 W-1 L / Unders 5 W-4 L" claim in this file's earlier September 6 section. Adopted as `L-102`.

### 2. Five settlement rows were recorded as missing when they were recoverable — recovered and settled

`P-288`, `P-290`, `P-291`, `P-292` and `P-293` were shown with dashes at Rank #2 and below in the settlement table above, even though their complete original ranked slates were preserved earlier in this same file (the raw component-import sections for each card). These are **not** the same kind of gap as `P-304`/`P-305` (`L-086`), where the rows genuinely never existed anywhere — these rows existed all along and were simply not carried forward into the summary table. They are now recovered and settled directly in the table above:

| Card | Recovered rows |
|---|---|
| `P-288` | Over 7.0 → **WIN** (13 runs); Under 7.0 → **LOSS**; Athletics +1.5 → **WIN** |
| `P-290` | 1st Half Over 0.5 → **LOSS** (goal was 53', 2nd half); Pachuca X2 → **WIN**; Pachuca most corners → **UNRESOLVED**; FT Over 2.5 → **LOSS** |
| `P-291` | Over 38.5 total games → **WIN** (45); Shelton -5.5 games → **LOSS** (margin only +5); Under 38.5 → **LOSS** |
| `P-292` | St Kilda +51.5 → **LOSS** (margin 92); North Melbourne -51.5 → **WIN**; Over 89.5 → **WIN** |
| `P-293` | Under 87.5 → **WIN** (31); Port Adelaide -13.5 → **LOSS**; Over 87.5 → **LOSS** |

Adopted as `L-103`, extending `G34.1`: check the raw preserved component text for a recoverable slate before marking any row `ARCHIVAL_OMISSION`.

### 3. The Rank #1/#2 comparison used an incomplete, unequal population — corrected

With the five recovered cards restored, the matched cohort with **both** Rank #1 and Rank #2 present grows from the previously reported 10 cards to the full **15**: `P-288`, `P-290`–`P-293`, `P-294`–`P-303`.

| Card | Rank #1 | Rank #2 | Card | Rank #1 | Rank #2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P-288 | L | W | P-296 | W | W |
| P-290 | W | L | P-297 | W | L |
| P-291 | W | W | P-298 | L | W |
| P-292 | L | L | P-299 | L | L |
| P-293 | W | W | P-300 | L | W |
| P-294 | L | W | P-301 | L | W |
| P-295 | L | L | P-302 | W | W |
| | | | P-303 | W | W |

**Corrected matched-cohort figures: Rank #1 7 WIN / 8 LOSS (46.7%); Rank #2 10 WIN / 5 LOSS (66.7%).** Both top two correct: **5 of 15** (`P-291`, `P-293`, `P-296`, `P-302`, `P-303`). Both top two wrong: **3 of 15** (`P-292`, `P-295`, `P-299`). This corrects the earlier "7 WIN/3 LOSS" Rank #2 figure and the earlier "3 of 10 / 2 of 10" joint figures, both of which were computed from the incomplete 10-card population. Adding `P-304` and `P-305` (Rank #1 only, both **WIN**) gives the full 17-card Rank #1 figure of **9 WIN / 8 LOSS** — this figure is confirmed unchanged by the correction; only the Rank #2 and joint figures were wrong. `C-RANK2-GAP`'s evidence base is updated accordingly in `LEARNING_REGISTER.md` §3A; the test's design, standing no-reordering constraint and 100-card/twelve-month completion criterion are unaffected. Adopted as part of `L-103`.

### 4. This session's own new gates (`G20.2`, `G21.1`, `G26.1`) reintroduced the exact problem the framework had just adopted a firewall to prevent

The predictive-weighting firewall (`L-087`), adopted earlier the same day in response to the first external audit, exists specifically to stop a same-session cohort finding from being written into a rule as an immediate hard ordinal bar. The second review found that this session's own `G20.2`, `G21.1` and `G26.1` — adopted that same day — each did exactly that:

- **`G20.2`** barred a `TAIL_EXPOSED` row from Rank #1 outright, and its tail-budget sum was described as "preventing an already-observed combination from recurring" — overclaiming that a sum of two different matches' order statistics represents a jointly observed event.
- **`G21.1`** classified any cumulative total below its median as a "union," conflating "easy to clear" (a difficulty fact) with "settles on any one discrete event" (a structural fact) — a category error that risked a built-in bias.
- **`G26.1`** barred a row inside a 40–60% reference split from Rank #1 outright, with no derivation for that specific band and no sample-adequacy requirement, based on a single card (`P-300`).

**All three are corrected in place, 2026-09-06(d), in `RULES_GENERAL.md` §15 (method now `MDS-2026.09.06-v3.9`):** the hard ordinal bars are withdrawn; each field becomes **disclosure only**, printed but not ordinal, with any ordinal consequence held in its own frozen prospective test (`C-TAIL-BUDGET`, `C-PATH-GEOMETRY`, and new `C-SEPARATION-FLOOR`). `G21.1`'s categories are corrected to `TRUE_UNION` / `LOW_BAR_CUMULATIVE` / `CENTRAL_BAND`. Adopted as `L-104`. Two pre-existing gates were also found to overclaim and are corrected the same pass: `G25.1`'s blanket ban on a `DISJOINT` row in the top half (a mathematically valid counterexample exists — three mutually exclusive outcomes at 0.45/0.40/0.15 can correctly rank #1 and #2; `L-108`), and `G30.1`'s claim that cushion strength "necessarily" transfers substantial weight to the outright-win branch (a counterexample with a 70%-cover cushion and an 80%-outright favourite coexisting is valid; `L-109`).

### 5. Nine sport-specific tail-budget formulas failed a dimensional check — corrected in every affected `RULES_<SPORT>.md`

Verified independently by reconstructing each formula against the actual settlement figures:

| Sport | Error | Corrected formula |
|---|---|---|
| AFL | Scoring shots × conversion rate gives **goals**, not points; behinds vanish | `points = S×(1+5p)` where `S` = shots, `p` = goals/`S`. Applied to `P-292`'s real 29 shots/14 goals: `29×(1+5×14/29) = 99`, matching the actual total exactly |
| NRL | Tries × kicking% omits the try points themselves and penalty/field goals | `points = 4×tries + 2×(tries×kicking%) + 2×penalty_goals` |
| Rugby union | Same tries/kicking shortcut, same omission | `points = 5×T + 2×(T×c) + 3×penalty_goals + 3×drop_goals` (+ penalty tries where applicable) |
| Ice hockey | Goals + power-play conversion **rate** mixes a count and a rate | Requires an actual expected power-play-opportunity **count** multiplied by the rate, not a bare rate added to a goal total |
| Basketball | Possessions × offensive rating omits the **/100** normalisation (rating is points per 100 possessions) | `points = (Poss × ORtg) / 100` |
| Soccer | Post-60' substitution window added as a term **on top of** the already-inclusive full-match total | Partition non-overlapping windows; never sum a sub-interval with a total that already contains it |
| Baseball | Starter-centre + relief-rate lacked an innings/exposure multiplier, and a "17 runs cannot be a starter misread" overclaim ignored the file's own `P-281` counterexample (early-inning starter failure) | Multiply each segment's rate by its actual innings exposure and conserve nine total innings; check both the early-failure and bullpen-tail branches, not just one |
| Tennis | Second-highest set count × median games per set misses close-set variation and legal-score-path constraints | Kept as a first-pass heuristic only; enumerate actual legal score paths when precision matters |
| American football | Points-per-drive × "drives allowed" lacked a coherent shared drive definition; the bench analogue was QB-only | Define "drive" identically for both sides before comparing; record offensive/defensive line rotation, not only the backup quarterback |

Adopted as `L-105`. `RULES_BASEBALL.md`'s entire September 6 section had also been lost from the working tree at some point after it was first written this session — it is restored here with the correction already incorporated, rather than restored verbatim and then corrected separately.

### 6. Reasoning, population and identity corrections

- **`P-291` (tennis, `F14`).** The preserved card text asserts "the cushion survives every Shapovalov win" — false in general in best-of-five tennis (a match winner can still lose the aggregate game count by a wide margin via a lopsided set split; a 0-6, 0-6, 7-6, 7-6, 7-6 winner has 21 games to the loser's 30). The actual settlement is correct (20+5.5=25.5>25); the card's *stated reasoning* contained a false general claim regardless. Annotated in `RULES_TENNIS.md`; reinforces `L-068` rather than superseding it. Adopted as `L-110`.
- **ETPL reference population (`F15`).** Re-verified against ESPN's own official match-number field (`competitions[].description`), retrieved again this session: the fixture labelled "Match 13" in the population table (Amsterdam Flames v Glasgow Cosmic, 72/1) is actually ESPN's **12th Match**; the genuine 13th Match is a different fixture (Glasgow Cosmic v Belfast Wolves, reduced to 12 overs, already correctly recorded in `P-286`) and is correctly excluded from a full-six-over population, not silently merged with the mislabelled entry. Corrected in `IMPROVEMENT_PLAN_2026-09-06.md`, `LEAGUE_RULES_CRICKET.md` and above. **Not resolved:** three earlier same-competition cards (`P-099`, `P-115`, `P-171`) could not be reconciled into this population — a repeat ESPN query for their date range (2026-08-09 to 2026-08-16) returned no events under this series ID. Flagged as an open population-completeness gap, not silently closed either way. Adopted as `L-111`.
- **Cricket "bimodal" claim (`F16`).** `L-083`'s bimodal-distribution claim rested on two observations (68/3, 55/1) — too thin to support a validated statistical shape. Narrowed in `RULES_CRICKET.md` to a wide-variance/resource-sensitive disclosure; the actionable requirement (retrieve wickets, name the collapse branch) is unchanged. Adopted as `L-112`.
- **`P-300` causal claim (`F19`).** The earlier text implied that a later match at the same venue producing higher powerplay scores helped establish that wickets *caused* `P-300`'s low score. Corrected: consistent with, not proof of; a different match cannot exclude alternative causes for an earlier, separate result. Softened in `RULES_CRICKET.md`.
- **`P-299` team-identity swap (`F22`).** The headline text and `L-076`'s own origin-evidence cell said Mali were missing five eventual finalists in the anchoring friendly; the correct, detailed retrospective (both here and in `RULES_BASKETBALL.md`) says **Spain** were missing five of Spain's own eventual World Cup rotation players, with Mali's Sika Koné separately absent from that friendly's box score. Corrected above and in `LEARNING_REGISTER.md`. Adopted as `L-116`.
- **`P-303` corridor overclaim (`F21`).** The retrospective claimed the actual result (81-53, total 134, margin 28) landed "almost exactly inside" a stated corridor (Nigeria 72-78, Hungary 64-71, total 136-149). It does not — it falls outside on all four checked bounds. Corrected above; the card's contract outcomes and ranking rationale are unaffected. Adopted as `L-115`.
- **`DATA_SOURCE_REGISTER.md` §6A (`F18`).** "Rung 6 ... cannot fail" is corrected to an attempted rung with an explicit `UNKNOWN` fallback and wider uncertainty, since a new venue or absent comparable data can defeat it. Adopted as `L-113`.
- **`G36.1` (`F27`).** Split into two separate fields, `RESEARCH_GRADE` (settled under stated standard rules) and `OPERATOR_ACTION` (`UNKNOWN_DEFINITION` where no operator ticket exists) — neither may be dropped or merged into the other. Adopted as `L-119`.
- **`G10.1` (`F26`).** Corrected to note that a structured field is not infallible or automatically independent of another endpoint from the same upstream vendor, and that an access failure on one route does not itself prove competition-wide non-coverage.

### 7. What was checked and found already adequate, with no new mechanism

`F05` (historical identity corrections, already fixed), `F17` (phase/format mismatches, already covered by existing sport-file controls), `F20` (named kill paths needing a checkable rank effect, already covered by `L-006`/`L-009`/`L-010` and others), `F23` (knowability timestamping, already a standing field), `F24` (bench/coaching already partially required pre-`G14.2` — `G14.2`'s origin story is corrected to "strengthened enforcement," but the gate itself is retained since it adds real value), `F25` (a list of already-adopted conditional heuristics, retained as a useful index), `F30` (already addressed by `L-090`'s stratification requirement, adopted earlier the same day). `F04`'s "12 vs 9" accounting discrepancy traced to a since-superseded interim count and needed no further correction beyond what `GAME_LOG_STATUS_INDEX_2026-09-05.md` already states.

### 8. What this pass did not do

- **No settled card's contract outcome (WIN/LOSS/PUSH) was changed.** Every correction here is to reasoning, disclosure, population completeness, gate wording, or previously-uncounted-but-recoverable rows — never to a result already recorded from an official final.
- **The full P-001–P-200 game-by-game table the second review supplied was not individually re-litigated.** Most of its entries restate or reinforce controls already covered in this framework; re-annotating roughly 200 historical cards one by one was not repeated here. The register instead absorbed the underlying *classes* of finding (`L-102`–`L-121`) so the same class is caught automatically going forward.
- **The `P-099`/`P-115`/`P-171` ETPL population gap was not resolved**, only honestly flagged — a confident reconstruction was not achievable within this pass without risking fabricated match identities.
- **The 86 pre-`L-095` lessons were not retroactively classified** under the new `BLOCKING_INTEGRITY`/`MECHANISM_REQUIRED`/`CONTEXT_MATERIALITY` taxonomy — that remains explicitly deferred future work.
- **This session did not treat the second review's findings as automatically correct.** Every numerical claim checked against primary source in this pass was independently re-derived and confirmed before any correction was made; nothing here was accepted on assertion alone.

## 2026-09-06(e) — comprehensive framework overhaul: reconciliation and settlement of the off-book `P-306`–`P-317` cohort

**Origin.** `FRAMEWORK_AND_GAME_LOG_OVERHAUL_REVIEW_2026-09-06.md` (a full review of every game log from `P-001` onward, requested and delivered the same day) found that twelve canonical IDs — `P-306` through `P-317` — had been issued into `PREDICTION_MINI_RUNNING_LOG_P317.md`, an external-session running log that was never reconciled into this file. Every statement in this snapshot and in `README.md` that "no event in the log is awaiting a result" was **false** at the time it was written: it did not account for a file that existed and was the most recently modified document in the repository. This section corrects that.

**What was done.** Each of the twelve cards' exact frozen contract terms was read from `PREDICTION_MINI_RUNNING_LOG_P317.md`. Each event's official result was then retrieved fresh from field-owning or two-independent-source records (cited per card below) and every row graded under the stated contract terms — no card's stated ranking, evidence label or reasoning was altered; only the settlement outcome was added. `P-316`/`P-317` are one event (`P-317` is `P-316`'s own same-event pregame revision, issued before first pitch); per the card's own instruction and `G1`/`L-014`, `P-316` is recorded as `CANONICAL_ALIAS_OF_P-317` with its slate preserved for archival completeness but not separately counted in performance. `P-307` (India W vs Pakistan W, Women's Asia Cup T20) reached no completed forecast — the user moved to the next query before a card was issued — and closes as `NO FORECAST ISSUED`, administrative, non-scorable, per §8's standing rule that no-forecast records are not trials.

**Corrected 2026-09-06(f): this is descriptive historical evidence, not an out-of-sample validation of the overhaul.** P-306, P-312 and P-315 explicitly describe start-crossed views; retain them separately from strict pregame trials. Complementary outcomes, differing line difficulty, mixed competitions and alternate thresholds prevent raw rank success rates from proving discrimination. Frozen issuance does not make an outcome-known cohort a prospective test of later rule changes. Full horizon-stratified counts and per-game reasoning are in [PREDICTION_MINI_LOG_SETTLEMENT_2026-09-06.md](archive/mini_logs/PREDICTION_MINI_LOG_SETTLEMENT_2026-09-06.md).

Adding this cohort to the all-history table in `FRAMEWORK_AND_GAME_LOG_OVERHAUL_REVIEW_2026-09-06.md` §2.1: **137-96 across 233 graded events (58.8%)**, retained as a previously published mixed-population descriptive aggregate. This pass has not rebuilt its all-history row/view joins; the hit rate alone cannot establish or disprove discrimination. The most recent 42 consecutive graded events (the last four cohorts plus this one) now stand at **19-23 (45.2%)**.

### Process note

This reconciliation is itself governed by the framework's own rules: no settled card's originally issued ranking, evidence label or contract wording was changed; only the settlement (a post-hoc fact) was added, exactly as every other settlement in this log has been recorded. `G34.1` (archival completeness) is satisfied — `P-316`'s complete frozen slate is preserved above rather than discarded on supersession. The ledger-integrity failure that produced this gap, and the structural fix adopted to prevent recurrence (a single canonical log with a 24-hour reconciliation rule for any external running log), are recorded in `METHOD.md` and `AUDIT_CHANGELOG_2026-09-06_OVERHAUL.md`.

### `P-294`–`P-317` — deeper `MDS-2026.09.06-v4.0` retrospective (2026-09-07 pass)

The 2026-09-06(e) reconciliation settled `P-306`–`P-317` and the 2026-09-06(f) addendum recovered rows and added per-game retrospectives for `P-288`–`P-317`. The 2026-09-07 pass applied the current three-question schema (`METHOD.md` §7.2 — driver / knowability / smallest routine change) to each of `P-294`–`P-317` and the stale carryovers `P-288`/`P-290`–`P-293`/`P-151`/`P-273`. No settled contract outcome, issued rank, evidence label or reasoning was changed. Full text: [`PREDICTION_MINI_LOG_SETTLEMENT_2026-09-06.md`](archive/mini_logs/PREDICTION_MINI_LOG_SETTLEMENT_2026-09-06.md) §§"Per-game retrospective — stale mini-log carryovers" and "Per-game retrospective — P-294 through P-317", and the byte-preserved component [`archive/mini_logs/PREDICTION_MINI_RUNNING_LOG_P317.md`](archive/mini_logs/PREDICTION_MINI_RUNNING_LOG_P317.md) §"2026-09-07 APPEND-ONLY SETTLEMENT / RETROSPECTIVE APPENDIX".

**Repeated sporting observations across the cohort (descriptive, no rule promoted — `L-087` firewall):** goals ≠ possession ≠ corners (`P-304`/`P-306`/`P-290`); strong early cricket scoring does not ensure a full-innings Over (`P-305`) and a slow start does not ensure an Under (`P-217`); winner / margin / total allocation need separate assessments (`P-309`, `P-312`–`P-315`); legal tennis game sums remain necessary (`P-291`/`P-308`/`P-310`); winning a wide cushion or a total can coexist with an inaccurate score corridor (`P-303`/`P-314`); historical batting roles can change materially (`P-311`). Administrative/validity corrections: scan frozen slates before declaring archival omission; scan unresolved sub-rows even when the event says settled; count research gaps separately from operator questions; preserve explicit start-crossed labels; check source lineage rather than counting hostnames; describe HTTP errors as scoped access evidence.

### Per-card retrospective (three-question schema, `METHOD.md` §7.2)

**`P-318` — Henan 0–0 Chengdu.** *Driver:* Chengdu produced possession with zero shots on target and zero corners; the "Felipe suspended → pressure without finishing" low-output branch happened far more strongly than the central attacking branch. *Knowable:* partly — Felipe's suspension, Henan's low-scoring home form and an explicit "territory, low finishing" branch were in the card; the extreme zero-corner/zero-SOT endpoint was not; the card over-weighted H2H first-half-goal frequency and historical Chengdu corner averages against the current striker/lineup uncertainty. *Smallest change:* when a major central finisher is absent and the current XI is incomplete, run one current route-to-corner / route-to-shot-on-target check before letting historical team-corner averages outrank the low-conversion branch. Research note, not a fitted coefficient.

**`P-319` — Yunnan 5–0 Liaoning.** *Driver:* Yunnan converted its home edge early (32', 40') then separated decisively; one-sided five-goal result despite Liaoning holding slightly more possession and six corners. *Knowable:* yes, directionally — the 7-2-3 home record vs Liaoning's 1-3-7 away, Liaoning's weak away scoring/defence, Kunimoto's suspension and a Yunnan high-goal branch were all in the card; the exact 5–0 and Cléber hat-trick were not. *Smallest change:* when a short recent low-score sample conflicts sharply with a season/home scoring environment, keep an explicit high-conversion favourite tail in the component budget. The corner Over won by the minimum possible margin (9 vs 8.5) while Yunnan itself took only three corners — goals, possession and total corners remain separate targets.

**`P-320` — Tianjin 2–1 Zhejiang.** *Driver:* the early-event / three-goal branch occurred but the allocation was wrong — Tianjin, not Zhejiang, converted the match into the stronger territory/corner and won it. *Knowable:* partly — the Tianjin home/relegation-pressure upset path, Zhejiang's poor away record, both strikers' absences and the exact corner kill path (efficient attacks end as shots/goals, not blocks) were in the card; exact XIs/benches were unresolved so the winner and team-corner conclusions were already evidence-capped. *Smallest change:* for team-corner rows, supplement season corner averages with a current opponent-conditioned route profile; do not treat general attacking strength or possession as sufficient evidence for a team-corner threshold.

**`P-321` — Sønderjyske 2–5 AC Horsens.** *Driver:* early scoring became a seven-goal game, but the scoring did not generate a high-corner environment; the event records also show a major first-half disciplinary disruption to Sønderjyske. *Knowable:* the strong first-half-goal and Horsens-winner paths were in the card; a specific red-card event was not; the corner forecast was built from ~11-corner historical match environments and did not survive the goal-rich / low-corner path. *Smallest change:* preserve a goal-rich / corner-poor branch in soccer derivative analysis, and flag red-card matches as *disrupted* when later using them for baseline learning. The red card is not retrospective proof the pre-game total should have been Over.

**`P-322` — South Africa 205/5 beat Zimbabwe by 48.** *Driver:* the opening pair immediately validated the high-phase branch (67/0), and SA sustained enough middle/death output to reach 205; Zimbabwe then failed to chase. *Knowable:* yes, unusually clearly — SA powerplays of 61 and 59 vs Zimbabwe plus 70 in the latest relevant game are why the PP Over was Rank 1; the 228 ceiling and a current batting-process argument supported the innings Over; the exact 67/0 and 205 were not knowable. *Smallest change:* none beyond retaining the existing phase-specific evidence chain. Positive process evidence: PP and full-innings targets were assessed *separately* and happened to align; this does not justify inferring full-innings Over from every strong powerplay.

**`P-323` — Everton 2–2 Manchester United (`EPL PRIMARY_SCORED`).** *Driver:* the game was entirely a second-half event — United scored at 46', then a late 83'/88'/90+6' exchange produced the Over and the draw; United had the attacking quality for two goals but only three corners. *Knowable:* partly — United's deeper attacking bench and both teams' defensive vulnerability were in the card, so a second-half high-event state was available; the 74% Rank-1 1H Over was too aggressive relative to the card's own low-event H2H evidence; the exact 46' breakthrough and stoppage-time equaliser were unknowable. *Smallest change:* when a first-half probability is high but the evidence set also contains multiple recent low-event H2Hs, show the phase split explicitly — first-half creation vs second-half substitution/score-state creation — and do not let full-match attacking quality silently inflate a first-half probability. **Worst-scored card of the cohort (mean Brier 0.3749).**

**`P-324` — Valencia 0–5 Barcelona.** `ADMINISTRATIVE CLOSED / NO FORECAST ISSUED.` Research began after the official kickoff and the exact live clock/period could not be verified, so no rank, probability or winner was issued — a **correct fail-closed** outcome. Smallest change is operational only: obtain an exact clock/period source faster if a live request is to be actionable. No W/L or Brier is created.

**`P-325` — Bangladesh Women 114/8; Sri Lanka Women won by 4 wkts.** *Driver:* Bangladesh's powerplay stayed below the line (37/1) and the full innings finished only 2.5 runs below 116.5; Sri Lanka then survived a difficult chase. *Knowable:* yes, directionally — the card explicitly preferred Bangladesh's slower powerplay and lower innings ceiling on current personnel/injury context; the exact 37/1 and 114/8 were not knowable, and the innings result was **boundary-sensitive**, not a dominant Under. *Smallest change:* preserve the existing phase/innings separation and label a 114-vs-116.5 result as a **narrow boundary validation**, not proof of a broad low-scoring regime.

**`P-326` — Campbell 28–19 Western Carolina.** `ADMINISTRATIVE CLOSED / NO ACTIONABLE FORECAST ISSUED.` The delivery crossed the rescheduled kickoff and the immediate delayed / zero-play state could not be jointly confirmed by the field owner, so the pregame ranking was withheld and no live view was manufactured. Fail-closed state gate retained.

**`P-327` — Angers 1–2 Rennes.** *Driver:* Rennes scored at 9' and benefited from a 17' own goal; Angers replied at 35'; all three goals before halftime, then Angers' sustained chase generated a very large corner count (18). *Knowable:* the early-goal, Rennes-attacking and high-corner branches were all present; the exact own goal was not. The total Over cleared by only one goal. *Smallest change:* none beyond explicitly preserving **trailing-team corner accumulation** as a mechanism separate from goal conversion. **Best-scored card of the cohort (mean Brier 0.1149).**

**`P-328` — Arsenal 2–1 Chelsea (`EPL PRIMARY_SCORED`).** *Driver:* Chelsea's 77-second opener created the high-tempo chase state; Arsenal produced heavy first-half pressure, equalised at 25', completed the comeback at 50'; the corner line cleared by one (5 vs 4.5). *Knowable:* yes, directionally — both current attacks, Chelsea's midfield absence, Arsenal's home edge and an early-goal route were all in the card; the first-77-seconds goal was not. *Smallest change:* no structural change; retain that an early goal changes the subsequent exposure state, and record that the corner row cleared **5 vs 4.5**.

**`P-329` — Bologna 2–2 Sassuolo.** *Driver:* high attempt/corner volume and two Sassuolo leads, then Bologna's bench (Dovbyk, 90+1') supplied the equaliser; Bologna's underlying attacking volume finally converted enough to break the preferred Under. *Knowable:* partly — the card knew Bologna's strong xG despite zero league goals, listed a credible high-event branch, and knew Dovbyk was on the bench and Sassuolo's defence/rotation was incomplete; the stoppage-time substitute goal was unknowable. The main process weakness was giving the Under 58% while the same evidence acknowledged substantial Bologna shot creation and capable attacking reserves. *Smallest change:* whenever a high-impact striker/creator is **available from the bench**, include that role explicitly in the second-half scoring component.

**`P-330` — Puerto Rico Women 64–76 Belgium Women.** `ADMINISTRATIVE CLOSED / NO ACTIONABLE FORECAST ISSUED.` No rank or winner was issued because the live-state gate could not be verified; no retrospective prediction or Brier score is fabricated.

**`P-331` — Cincinnati 12–8 Milwaukee (`MLB PRIMARY_SCORED`).** *Driver:* Cincinnati's offence overwhelmed the Brewers in the middle innings — De La Cruz drove in four, Cincinnati stacked multiple-hit performances, and Milwaukee defensive errors compounded the run environment; Singer allowed six runs but was carried by twelve. *Knowable:* partly — the card had GABP homer volatility, Cincinnati's right-handed matchup path, Harrison's severe prior-start HR failure and a ~10-run upper state, but still leaned Milwaukee 64% on the stronger season/starter profile; the exact De La Cruz production, error sequence and 20-run final were unknowable. *Smallest change:* in hitter-friendly parks, keep **defence/error and multi-inning bullpen exposure** as separate run components rather than letting the starting-pitcher comparison dominate the winner allocation. Not a blanket Over rule.

**`P-332` — Alavés 5–2 Osasuna.** *Driver:* Alavés turned an even opening into a 2–0 halftime lead, Osasuna responded immediately after the break, and the match expanded to seven goals (Boyé hat-trick); despite the goal explosion, only five corners. *Knowable:* partly — the card had Alavés' strong current xG, Osasuna's Budimir threat and a 2–1/1–2 upper-tail branch, but made low-event 0–0/1–0/1–1 families central and put Under 2.5 at 60%; the conflicted Alavés lineup evidence should have *widened* the tails rather than supporting a narrow low-event centre; the exact hat-trick and seven-goal output were unknowable. *Smallest change:* when both teams have credible attacking xG/finishing routes and the participant state is conflicted, widen the score-distribution tail explicitly before assigning a 60% Under. Separately: seven goals occurred with only five corners — do not infer corners from goal volume.

### Descriptive settlement summary

Issued sporting cards: **12** (`P-318`, `P-319`, `P-320`, `P-321`, `P-322`, `P-323`, `P-325`, `P-327`, `P-328`, `P-329`, `P-331`, `P-332`). Administrative no-forecast closures: **3** (`P-324`, `P-326`, `P-330`). Ranked rows settled: **57 = 28 W / 29 L**. Rank #1: **9 W / 3 L**. Potential-winner calls: **7 W / 5 L**.

Mixed sports, mixed horizons, mixed target families. The raw 28/57 and 9/12 are **descriptive only** — not a model accuracy claim, ROI, edge or calibration estimate. Complementary Over/Under pairs, alternate thresholds and differing contract difficulty make raw rank success rates mechanically dependent (`L-055`). Frozen issuance does not make an outcome-known cohort a preregistered prospective test of the v4.0 changes. The all-history Rank #1 descriptive aggregate published at §"2026-09-06(e)" (137-96 across 233 graded events, 58.8%) is **not** rebuilt here — this cohort's 9-3 is recorded descriptively and continues to sit within the same non-discriminative band.

### v4.0 Brier / calibration scorecard — first scored cohort

Seven of the fifteen `P-318`–`P-332` records carried scored `UNVALIDATED_SUBJECTIVE` probability slates (`P-323`, `P-325`, `P-327`, `P-328`, `P-329`, `P-331`, `P-332`); `P-318`–`P-322` predated consistent probability issuance in the mini log and are graded W/L only; `P-324`/`P-326`/`P-330` issued nothing.

| Population | Cards | Scored binary rows | W / L | Mean Brier (method) | 0.5 baseline |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| **EPL — `PRIMARY_SCORED`** | `P-323`, `P-328` | 10 | 5 / 5 | **0.2666** | 0.2500 |
| **MLB — `PRIMARY_SCORED`** | `P-331` | 4 | 2 / 2 | **0.1893** | 0.2500 |
| **NRL / AFL — `PRIMARY_SCORED`** | — | 0 | — | — | — |
| Combined `PRIMARY_SCORED` (this cohort) | 3 | 14 | 7 / 7 | **0.2445** | 0.2500 |
| `EXPLORATORY` (`P-325`, `P-327`, `P-329`, `P-332`) | 4 | 19 | 10 / 9 | **0.1987** | 0.2500 |
| **All scored rows (mixed)** | 7 | **33** | **17 / 16** | **0.2181** | **0.2500** |

Calibration table (all 33 scored rows, mixed population; buckets below 0.50 are mostly the contrary/complement row on each card — a mirror of its pair, not independent evidence):

| Probability bucket | Rows | Wins | Win rate |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 0.70–0.80 | 3 | 2 | 67% |
| 0.60–0.70 (incl. 0.60) | 7 | 5 | 71% |
| 0.55–0.60 | 8 | 6 | 75% |
| 0.50–0.55 | 1 | 1 | 100% |
| 0.45–0.50 | 2 | 0 | 0% |
| 0.40–0.45 | 8 | 2 | 25% |
| 0.25–0.35 | 4 | 1 | 25% |
| **Total** | **33** | **17** | **52%** |

**Sample size, stated prominently: 33 scored rows across 7 cards and ~6 competitions, with heavy within-card dependence.** Nothing here supports a calibration claim, a superiority claim over the 0.5 baseline, or any statement about discrimination. The mixed-population mean (0.2181) beats the baseline by 0.032; the `PRIMARY_SCORED` subset (0.2445) is essentially at the baseline. The current `PRIMARY_SCORED` card count is **3**; the pattern/calibration review runs at the 25-card cadence (`METHOD.md` §7.2). This scorecard is carried forward as the running total in `PREDICTION_LOG_COMBINED_3.md`'s snapshot; every `P-333`+ card adds to it.

### Candidate watch items opened (not promoted rules)

Each needs 3+ recurrences in a 25-card `PRIMARY_SCORED` window before it can become a `CANDIDATE` control (`L-087` firewall). **No new predictive rule, weight, ordinal bar or fitted coefficient is promoted from this cohort.**

- current route-to-corner / route-to-shot-on-target evidence when a major central attacker is absent (`P-318`);
- explicit second-half bench-attacker scoring contribution (`P-329`);
- distance-to-line recorded in every derivative retrospective evaluation (`P-319`, `P-325`, `P-328`);
- a "disrupted-match" flag (red card / GK dismissal / long weather delay) for later population-baseline learning (`P-321`, `P-234`, `P-309`).

**No settled card's contract outcome (WIN/LOSS/PUSH) was changed. No probability was minted retroactively.**

# Appendix — unsettled and incomplete logs

Per user directive (2026-09-07): every unsettled or incomplete log inherited from Parts 1 and 2 is collected here, at the bottom of Part 2, **using lettering as its identifier** (its canonical `P-###` ID is preserved alongside). Each entry keeps its **existing learnings** and records a **maximum fresh settlement attempt** made on 2026-09-07. `PREDICTION_LOG_COMBINED_3.md` does **not** carry these items and does **not** attempt to settle them — this appendix is their sole home. If a field owner or operator resolution ever appears, settle the item here, in its own historical source record, and note it as an appendix update (never as a new `P-333`+ forecast).

**2026-09-07 maximum-attempt summary (applies to items B, C, E, F, G, J, K, L — the eight open derivative corner rows).** Routes tried: ESPN structured lane by direct query (`site.api.espn.com`, curl) — `chn.fa` / `chn.fa_cup` / `chn.china_fa_cup` → HTTP 400 (no Chinese-cup feed); `fra.3` / `fra.national` → HTTP 400 (no French third-tier feed); ESPN has no MLS Next Pro, no SFA "A" Division, no Liga MX Femenil feed. FotMob API (`/api/matches`, `/api/matchDetails`) → HTTP 404 (endpoint changed / protected). `api.sofascore.com` → 403 on every route (known, `LEARNING_REGISTER.md`). WebFetch of Flashscore, FotMob, Tribuna, worldfootball.net, matchendirect.fr → JS-rendered stat panels not present in the fetched content, or HTTP 403. WebSearch → returns the confirmed **scores and goal sequences** for every match but **no corner totals** for any of them. **Conclusion:** these competitions have no reachable field-owning structured corner provider and none is retrievable through any available tool. Under `G10.2`/`L-081` a secondary-aggregator count does not settle a derivative row to this framework's standard, so each row keeps its prior best-available disposition, explicitly labelled. This is the definitive maximum attempt; further re-tries are not expected to change anything unless one of these providers adds a public feed.

### Existing learnings for the appendix items (preserved verbatim in intent from the settlement mini log)

- **A / `P-126`:** without an authenticated event and final, a tactical explanation would be fabricated. The original identity uncertainty is the auditable issue; neither the old claimed 1–0 nor a neighbouring fixture can repair it. Smallest improvement: retain one explicit fixture-identity request for the organiser (teams, competition, date, venue); resume only when a source resolves that specific link. No model lesson or performance trial is created.
- **B / `P-148`:** chances and goalkeeper interventions did not produce an early goal; the winner arrived through a late penalty. Keep timing, finishing and corner-generation separate; do not turn the team's win into validation of its corner prediction.
- **C / `P-149`:** the open scoring and Ventura win fit the frozen attacking branch; a five-goal match does not establish Ventura's corner exposure. Retain the goal grades and request only the missing complete corner field.
- **D / `P-166`:** the competitive/high-total branches were printed; the period-by-period reversal (3–0 lead after two periods → 4–4) was not. Show regulation and completed-game endpoints together whenever they differ and identify which rows are invariant. Do not infer a goalie or fatigue cause from the score alone.
- **E / `P-176`:** all goals arrived after halftime, defeating both the attacking-ceiling and timing assumptions. Compare shot creation and finishing separately before treating a streak as a team-total limit. Corner confirmation remains a narrow evidence task.
- **F / `P-178`:** two goals by one team permit a winning match Under and a losing team Under simultaneously; a goal originating at a corner establishes at least one corner, not the final total. Evaluate the two-team score allocation explicitly and keep the derivative field open.
- **G / `P-179`:** the low-scoring allocation survived, but scoring at least once did not imply an early goal or an outright win. Give a draw its own winner outcome and make first-half exposure explicit.
- **H / `P-200`:** the previous dominant Herning result and roster-depth case did not produce separation here. Separate win probability from the margin required to cover; show the endpoint sensitivity alongside the grade.
- **I / `P-217`:** the slow start was followed by exceptional late scoring (Hetmyer 111\*, 85 runs in the last four overs). Retain a resource-conditioned late-acceleration branch; distinguish "six overs" from a competition's revised mandatory powerplay. The source card's external betting-line anchor is historical methodology, not market-blind v4 evidence.
- **J / `P-233`:** the favourite's attacking case succeeded while its clean-sheet case failed. Keep team attack and opponent scoring floor distinct; do not use a provisional corner total to claim the territory mechanism was validated.
- **K / `P-234`:** an early goalkeeper dismissal is a material post-issue state change — this match cannot cleanly estimate a general cup/knockout scoring-suppression effect. Retain the dismissal branch in the event-state framework; mark this as a **disrupted match** in any baseline comparison.
- **L / `P-235`:** the game total was cleared entirely by one side. Reconcile each side's scoring floor independently from the match total, retaining a one-sided three-goal branch. The corner mechanism remains unassessed until the exact statistic is recovered.
- **M / `P-274`:** the starter correction and bullpen-game structure were known; the exact two-hit outcome was not. Identify the reliever chain and the underdog's offensive floor separately. A bullpen game expands uncertainty but does not mechanically favour an underdog cushion or an Over.
- **N / `P-307`:** maintain a clear no-issuance disposition and carry any unfinished research as context only. A subsequent result cannot create a historical prediction.

## Closed exceptions remain separate

P-166, P-200, P-217 and P-274 keep their inherited research settlements and OPERATOR_ACTION = UNKNOWN_DEFINITION. Operator-specific OT/shootout, shortened-innings and listed-pitcher terms cannot be reconstructed from sporting results. They are four conditional operator follow-ups, not four live matches and not four additional open corner rows. P-003's old corner is terminal unsettleable; P-316 is an alias of P-317. Neither is silently re-opened. P-366's fixed 20-over rows are terminal censored, not extrapolated from a 19-over game.

## Next-pass process

Start with the live/identity check for each queued item, record exact source and UTC retrieval time, then move on if still live. On a final, adjudicate each frozen target independently. Retain unresolved corner/provider requirements as such; never select a new provider merely because it produces a desired grade. Retire a temporary handle only when its specific evidence condition is met or an explicit terminal disposition is documented. Add outcome-specific retrospective evidence at that point; process failures can and should be documented now.
